# Decisions and findings log

Status: LOG. Append-only; one line per entry; newest last.

Format: `| id | date | kind | area | statement | evidence |`. Ids are stable (d001, d002, ...) and never reused; new entries
take the next id and go at the end. kind is DECISION, FINDING, DROPPED or LESSON; area is product, engine, retrieval, eval,
process or infra. A statement carries its number and date. Evidence is a commit sha, a repo-relative results path, a
description of a private run record (work area, not in this repository), or "owner, chat <date>". "roadmap@bbd3292 <ID>" means
the text of that item in `git show bbd3292:docs/development/roadmap.md`, the last version of the old roadmap that held the
full result paragraphs. Numbers in this log are quoted from there or from the named record; the log does not repeat full tables.

Entries are in date order up to d337; later entries were added afterwards and keep the next free id whatever their date. Everything before 2026-10-17 was moved here from the old roadmap, the run checkpoints and the
result files when the documentation was cleaned up on 2026-10-17.

| d399 | 2026-10-20 | DECISION | infra | K1b landed: every `run` line of `lci.runrecord/0` carries a required `profile` object (`hash` and `keys` from `Config::run_profile()`, `embedding_identity_sha256`, and 16-hex `priors_hash` and `tie_break_hash` of the configured files or null), built by `lci_maps::run_record::profile_stamp`. | d402 | 2026-10-20 | DECISION | infra | K0a, part 1: when the embedding service is unavailable (refused or failed connection, timeout, dropped connection, HTTP 5xx: `models::is_service_unavailable`) and there are no real vectors to lose (the vector cache is empty and the previous snapshot, if any, has the same embedding identity), indexing commits the chunks without vectors (`Store::replace_vectorless`: one placeholder per chunk, table metadata `vectors = "missing"`, the real identity kept) instead of failing. `Status` and `IndexReport` report `vectors_missing`; `Status.embedding_dimension` is null then. The store refuses every vector read of such a snapshot (nearest search, stored vector, vector cache), and a search on it turns the semantic channel off with a warning, so lexical and LSP answer and traverse seeds from lexical. Any other embedding failure stays a hard error: an HTTP 4xx or bad response (configuration or protocol), a previous snapshot with vectors, or an identity change. Measured: one refused-port index run costs about 9 s on Windows (three attempts with 1 s and 2 s backoff); there is no embedder connect breaker, so part 2 (repair on search when the service returns) must throttle or probe before it retries. |
| d403 | 2026-10-20 | DECISION | ci | Owner direction: the CI `test` job runs on Windows only, to save runner time; the Linux and macOS test runs (continue-on-error since d381) are removed and return in cycle 2. The `minimum-version` and `canary` jobs stay: they check the declared and the newest Rust toolchains, not platform support. |
| d404 | 2026-10-20 | DECISION | infra | K0a landed with part 2. An embedder connect breaker (`EMBEDDER_DOWN_WINDOW`, 30 s, like the reranker's): any refused connection trips it; while it is open, query embeddings skip at once (the search fails open) and an index run makes one attempt without retries or backoff, so a restarted service is never refused untried. A vectorless snapshot is repaired by a search only under `freshness = on-search`, gated by the stale-check throttle, skipped without re-indexing while the breaker is open, and at most once per 30 s after a failed repair; a search that waited for another index run does not repeat it. `manual` and `watch` repair only through an explicit index or the watcher. Traverse `search.vector` on a vectorless snapshot sends no embedding request and records the reason. Measured on Windows: a refused-port index run costs about 9 s (three attempts, 1 s and 2 s backoff); under the open breaker an index run costs one refused connect (about 2 s) and a search about 0.1 s. Deferred to the M0 filler list: lci-maps `vector_of` over-counts one external call on that skip, and the hidden `with_legacy_run` record path builds its semantic map from the unadjusted channels. |
| d405 | 2026-10-20 | DECISION | infra | K35a: the dependency policy is written in docs/development/testing.md ("Dependency policy"): reuse proven libraries under the code licences the project accepts; pin a `0.0.x` crate exactly; heavy third-party code is an optional dependency behind an off-by-default Cargo feature, so the default build does not change; a change to the default build is said in the commit and checked with `git diff Cargo.lock`. `Cargo.lock` holds no `0.0.x` crate today, so the pin rule changed no manifest. |
| d406 | 2026-10-20 | DECISION | ci | K34a: `cargo-deny` (`deny.toml`) checks advisories, licences, bans and sources in a non-blocking CI job `deny` (cargo-deny-action, SHA-pinned). The licence allow-list holds the 14 licences the tree uses (permissive families plus MPL-2.0); GPL/LGPL families are added when a crate needs one. The workspace crates are private (`publish = false` on every member; `[licenses.private] ignore = true`), so LCI still has no licence file. Its first run found RUSTSEC-2026-0346 to 0349 in rustls 0.23.45; `Cargo.lock` moves to rustls 0.23.46, which fixes all four (one crate changed). Two unmaintained-crate warnings are ignored with reasons: `paste` (build-time proc macro) and `unsafe-libyaml` (reached only through a tokenizer LCI does not use). `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` lists every third-party crate with its licences, generated by cargo-deny (the command is in its header). |
| d407 | 2026-10-20 | DECISION | test | K7a landed: the fake LSP server has a `documents` mode that answers from the documents it was sent, not from disk: a start-up walk of the root, `didOpen`/full-sync `didChange` with version checks, `didClose` back to the last snapshot, and `didChangeWatchedFiles` re-reading the disk at event time. A per-line toy parser answers workspace symbols, definition, references, document symbols, implementation, and call and type hierarchy; call-hierarchy prepare resolves the identifier at the position to its declaration and answers nothing off an identifier, as real servers do. Every message is logged in order to `events.jsonl` (with the request subject and a per-process spawn ordinal) and every ignored or odd notification carries a note, so a missing, late or reordered notification shows in the answers and the log. All nine tests fail when the store is bypassed. Known limits for later fixtures: ranges cover the name token only (multi-line end ranges go to K10a), comments and strings are tokenised, TS methods need a modifier. Other fake modes are unchanged. |
| d408 | 2026-10-20 | DECISION | maps | K23a landed: `[run_profile.walk] groups = "tool" | "claim"` (default tool, today's numbers) and `grep_corroboration` (0..=1, default 1.0). Under claim, edges that assert one claim count once: the claim key is (expanding node, target, family), with families Refers (calls and imports together), Structure, Implements, Mentions, Tests, ReadsKey, History, Near, Other; map and witness are not in the key, and ids unify only when the maps resolve to the same node id (lsp.live and grep.live resolve to SCIP ids; their file-node fallbacks stay separate claims until K10a). An edge is worth the noisy-OR over its claims, the rank boost counts groups instead of maps, and the score bound is off (the answer says so). `grep_corroboration` is what a Mentions-only group adds. Claim with rrf fusion is a validation error. Handles keep the grouping (old handles load as tool); recipes walk under the run profile. The default profile hash moves only because the two leaves exist (a4898... to c10fe...). |
| d409 | 2026-10-20 | DECISION | lsp | N19 and N24 fixed (M0 filler). `[java] args` is split: JVM options (`-X`, `-D`, `-javaagent:`, `--add-*`, `--enable-native-access`, `@argfiles`, the assertion flags matched exactly or in their `:` form, and the others listed in java.rs) go before `-jar`, everything else after `-data <dir>`; a relative `[java] path` resolves against the LCI process's working directory, like `priors`. C# project discovery skips linked directories (symlinks and junctions). The Equinox launcher jar is the unversioned one, else the highest version (numeric components, then the qualifier as text). On Windows a server named without an extension resolves across PATH to `name.exe`, then `name.cmd` (PATHEXT is not consulted), and a relative path with a directory part comes back absolute; a configured `.cmd` path already worked. An explicit `[java] jvm_args` field could replace the heuristic later. |
| d410 | 2026-10-20 | DECISION | maps | K2a part 2 landed: `[run_profile.traverse] map_freshness` (default false: the answer is unchanged) adds `maps[]` to a traverse answer, one `{id, observed, stale, lag}` per enabled map that states a freshness. SCIP-built maps are `snapshot`; `lag` counts language source files newer than the index file, plus files changed, added or removed since this process first saw the index stamp, each once; `stale` is lag above 0; `lag` is null with a note when the call has no tree, the tree cannot be read or the index has no modified time, so "stale false" never stands for "not known". Live maps (grep.live, lsp.live, git.history, exact.pickaxe, probe.compile) are `live`; grep.live and lsp.live still place results by the index's positions, so a line shift is not covered by that label. Not seen: a change that left a file older than the index before the first sighting, and an edit that keeps the content hash. Cost with the key on: every call reads and hashes the tree (a stat-gated re-hash is on the M0 filler list). The run-profile pins move for the new leaf (default hash 854c4...). |
| d411 | 2026-10-20 | DECISION | maps | K20a landed: `[run_profile.scip] mode = authoritative | cache | off` (default authoritative: today's behaviour, the index is required). `cache` reads the index when the file is there and readable, else the SCIP graph is empty with a note; `off` never touches the index path (no read, no stat, no canonicalize). Under an empty graph, graph.scip, structure.contains beyond chunk lines, scip.impact, scip.implements, config.key and exact.closure are empty, each with a note naming the mode; grep.live places results by file only and lsp.live gives no edges (no place table: K10a). A handle made with a graph does not resume under off; under cache the identity still canonicalizes the path. Recipes follow the mode (the stamp is what ran): they read the index under cache when it is there and refuse under off or without it. Deferred to K20b: remembering a parse failure under cache. The run-profile pins move for the new leaf (default hash e4cab...). |
| d412 | 2026-10-20 | DECISION | maps | N20 (c) and part of (d) fixed (M0 filler). (c): a watcher pass queued for the workspace write lock ends on `unwatch` (`index_if_stale` takes the cancellation token; a biased select drops the queued lock wait); a pass that already holds the lock still runs to its commit, like an explicit index. (d): the file lock was fixed by N91; the `probe.compile` scratch-checkout gate is now a `try_lock` loop bounded by the query budget, the budget is re-derived after the gate and after the scratch copy (no copy and no cargo run when none is left), and a probe's problem is per call, so one busy moment no longer marks later responses Partial. | d413 | 2026-10-20 | DECISION | maps | K2a part 1 landed: the `Freshness` suite (tests/cases/freshness.rs) edits a fixture tree and queries every map but probe.compile, under on-search, manual, a line shift and a SCIP rewrite, with `map_freshness` on. Each map is Fresh (it saw the edit), StaleLabelled (its label says stale with a lag equal to the source files behind, and it still answered) or a listed Gap; `EXPECTED_GAPS` and `EXPECTED_LABELLED` are asserted in both directions, so a fix or a regression both fail until the list moves. grep.live and lsp.live are checked per generation with a probe symbol only the new tree has. | d424 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | lsp | K7b part 2 landed: `[run_profile.lsp] outline_cache` (default false: every outline lsp.live needs is a `textDocument/documentSymbol` request, as before). With `true` the language-server manager keeps each non-empty outline under the file's content hash (the index's sha-256) and the server session that answered, and answers the same unchanged file from memory; an edited file, or any file after the server restarted, was released, replaced or stopped idle, is asked again; an empty outline or an error is never kept. Only servers that hold a file's current text at every request use it: pyright, gopls, jdtls and clangd, whose adapters re-send the file in `didOpen` before each request; typescript-language-server refuses a repeated open, so it goes to K7c (document lifecycle). Four unit tests pin it (tau switch, offered-not-adopted `found`, estimate picking and `fraction <= 1`, the bound's empty answer) | K29 commit |

| d428 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | security | K37c: `probe.compile` (which runs `cargo check`, and so the tree's build scripts and procedural macros) is registered for a traverse call only under the existing process-wide grant `rust_analyzer_trusted = true`; without it a call that names the map gets a skipped note in `notes` and `warnings` and runs its other maps. Under the grant the answer notes that build scripts execute and the service logs one warning per process. Cargo always runs with `--offline`, `CARGO_NET_OFFLINE=true` and emptied compiler and Cargo wrapper variables; these do not confine build scripts, so the grant is the control. No sandbox without Docker at this size; the grant is reused, not renamed or split, since it already means the owner trusts the tree to run code (GPT-6 Sol review agreed). | d430 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | tests | Three test-infrastructure fixes (N57, N81, N82). N57: the live-acceptance cleanup (`xtask/src/live_acceptance.rs` and the workflow sweep step) no longer deletes every `lci-*` temp directory. Each run now puts its fixtures under one run-unique root (`lci-live-run-<pid>-<stamp>`, or `lci-live-run-<run id>-<attempt>` under `RUNNER_TEMP` in the workflow) passed to the steps as `LCI_ACCEPTANCE_FIXTURE_ROOT` and removes only that directory, so parallel runs and other worktrees are untouched (`Fixture::create` falls back to the temp directory when the variable is unset; a unit test pins which directories are removed; the workflow YAML is not runnable locally). N81: the test helper `process_is_running` checks the exit status and stderr of `tasklist` (Windows) and `ps` (Unix; exit 1 with no output means gone) and panics with the program output on a failed query instead of reading it as "not running". N82: Windows `finish_holder_test` fails when a spawned server has no sleeper pid file after a bounded `LOADED_BUDGET_SECONDS` wait, instead of skipping the ordinal, and accepts only a cancelled `JoinError` from the aborted call. Also two `init` refusal tests in `tests/cases/config.rs` bounded wall time at 30 s and failed under parallel builds; their "refused" status is decided from the URL string with no outbound probe, so the bound is now a hang guard of twice `LOADED_BUDGET`. | this commit |
| d432 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | walk | K24a: `log_odds` is a third value of the existing `[traverse] fusion` leaf (`boost` stays the default), not a new `walk.fusion` key: the leaf already names the fusion in the profile, the handle and the recipe stamp, so a second key would give one switch two homes. Capped log-odds over the sources `rrf` uses (one per independent map, the dependent maps together, a seed's own map): `score = sum over sources of weight * clamp(ln(p / (1 - p)), -3, 3)`, `p` the best potential the source delivered the node with, `p` held in (1e-6, 1 - 1e-6), and a source that delivered other results but not this node costs `weight * 3` (`LOG_ODDS_CAP`, a named constant). Silence costs the cap rather than 0 because at 0 two weak sources always beat one strong one, which is `rrf` again. The score bound is off and ties go by node id, as `rrf`; the handle writes `fusion` when it is not `boost` (old handles read unchanged); `claim` grouping refuses it as it refuses `rrf`. No default change: the run-profile golden does not move. The cap is a constant until a measurement asks for another value (cycle 2 screening). The map weight enters twice (in the hop potential and as the multiplier), so a map at weight 0.5 or less never adds a positive term on a first hop: its deliveries only cost less than silence; a delivery at potential 0 counts as silence. | K24a commit |
| d433 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | lsp | K8a: `[run_profile.lsp] sync`, default `"off"` (a language server walks the tree when it starts and is told nothing after, as before). With `"watch"` a long-lived language-server manager (the MCP and REST server's, which navigation, the search channel and `sessions = "shared"` traversals use; the one `lci traverse` builds under `"shared"`) starts an `lsp::sync::WorkspaceSync` with a workspace's first session and stops it with the last (release, shutdown, idle sweep, discard): a `notify` watcher of the root (ReadDirectoryChangesW, inotify, FSEvents; polling at `watch_poll_milliseconds` when it fails), opened and walked on blocking threads, whose events are drained into one `workspace/didChangeWatchedFiles` per batch for every session of the workspace, each source file typed by the disk (created, changed, deleted) against a `(mtime, length)` table from one `chunk::source_files` walk (walked again only after a reported overflow or watcher error). Every request drains what was already reported first; nothing waits on a walk. Lock order: drain lock, then the session registry (released before sending), then `syncs`. A synced change drops the changed files' outline-cache entries in that workspace (d424). Per-call managers never sync (their servers start on the current tree and die with the walk), so under `sessions = "per_call"` the key changes only the profile hash. Known limits: on Windows `notify` 8.2 silently stops watching a directory when ReadDirectoryChangesW fails with a buffer overflow (N102); single events are not filtered by `.gitignore`; a renamed directory arrives as the directory alone, so the files inside are not sent; prose files are not sent (K12c). Default off because it adds a watcher thread and a walk per synced workspace and changes what long-lived sessions answer, and only the shared and service sessions gain from it. New dependency `notify` (CC0-1.0). | K8a commit; Fable review |
| d435 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | eval | K3a-1 (xtask only, no product change): `maps bench` writes the cost of each traverse item next to its metrics and the cost per stratum, and `maps compare` leaves out any item a budget cut. `items.json` records gain `external_calls`, `physical_external_calls`, `lsp_ms` (milliseconds of the `lsp.live` map, 0 when it did not run), `answer_tokens` (chars/4 of the scored result texts; the set total of the trend row is now their sum) and, only when a budget cut the walk, `budget_hit` (`pops`, `tokens`, `external_calls`, `deadline`, `lsp_deadline`), read from the reply's `stop_reason` and `notes` (the engine ends a walk on `budget` only for tokens or an unadopted result, and a spent external-call allowance never ends a walk: the walk goes on with the maps that need no external call and the reply says so in a note; so `budget` is `tokens` always, and `external_calls` is set from that note alone, when no `pops`, `budget` or `deadline` stop ended the walk first; the note is also written as `external_spent: true` on the record whatever ended the walk). `score.json` gains a `costs` block (`overall` and `strata.<dimension>.<value>`: `n`, mean expansions, external calls, `lsp.live` ms and answer tokens, `budget_hits` counts) beside the scorer's untouched `strata`; `supplement.md` tables it per plan dimension. `ItemFlags.budget_hit` joins `any()`, so `maps compare` excludes budget-hit items by default (including every pop-capped walk) and `--keep-failed` keeps them; the exclusion counts are printed, in total and per kind and side; `maps compare` also compares `external_calls`, `lsp_ms` and `answer_tokens` by default. Old runs stay readable (missing keys are undefined for that metric, no `costs` block means no table); the trend row format and every scorer are unchanged. `sim` runs flag a pop-capped or token-capped `lci` call in `meta.json` `flagged_items` from now on. | d431 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | seeds | K21a-1 landed: `[run_profile.seeds] bm25` (default false) and the opt-in map `seeds.bm25`. With `true` the map joins the default set of a call that names no maps and runs when named, needing no `--root`. It is BM25 over the SCIP graph's function and method nodes, in process, with no new crate and the existing `lexical::score_chunks` unchanged: each node is a document of three fields (tokens of its name, of its qualified path (package, modules, types, member and the file's path) and of its documentation), a field that holds a question term is one credited line, so tf is the number of fields (1 to 3), document frequency counts nodes, and length is the node's token count; a term of at most 4 characters must be a whole token, a longer one may sit inside a token (`WORD_MATCH_MAX_CHARS`). Merge (coordinator decision B): the top `seed_k` nodes the hybrid search did not already give are appended after its nodes (`seed_source` `hybrid+bm25`, `seed_hits` `how: "bm25"`), or are the start nodes when the hybrid search gave none (`bm25`, with the reason in `seed_fallback`; the name-token stand-in then does not run); no RRF fusion here (K24a). Decision D accepted: `query_terms` drops question words under 3 characters. The documentation of a node is read from the SCIP index file only when the map first runs for an index stamp (`read_symbol_docs`: a second parse that copies each function symbol's lines cut at 1024 bytes, on a blocking thread, with the entity index built there too); the graph, `read_documents` and every load with the key off read and keep none, so the key off costs nothing. `RawDocument` is unchanged, so the roughly 40 literals of it stay as they are. `seeds.bm25` is classified as a SCIP-built map for `map_freshness`. The recipes' seeds are given, so they run the same. The run-profile golden gains `run_profile.seeds.bm25 = false`; the MCP schema goldens list no map ids and did not move. Not verified: whether `rust-analyzer scip` writes `documentation` (if none, the doc field is empty and names and paths carry the ceiling); the ceiling itself is K21a-2 (benches), with the `symbols` and `rg --fixed-strings` baseline arms folded into K5a. | K21a-1 commit |
| d434 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | lsp | K9b: `lsp.live` asks the call hierarchy of each symbol node under the new bool key `[run_profile.lsp] call_hierarchy` (default `false`; one key for both directions, planned decision A; no enum): `textDocument/prepareCallHierarchy`, then `callHierarchy/incomingCalls` and `callHierarchy/outgoingCalls` of the item that answers (sent back verbatim; its place is the `selectionRange`), each request behind the uniform `found.calls < max_calls` gate (B) and counted, so up to three more external calls per node and none after an empty prepare. Callers and callees are `CallsOrRefs` edges (d397: no new `EdgeKind`), witness `Exact`, one per destination per expansion (a destination that `definition` or `references` named keeps that edge), seen at the smallest place of the call (`fromRanges`). | d436 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | walk | K24b: `[run_profile.walk] pricing = "rank" | "relative"` (new leaf, default `rank` = today's arithmetic, so no default change; the default profile golden gains the line `run_profile.walk.pricing = "rank"` and the hash moves only for that, 2778289b... to 73e45557...). Above the gate (8 candidates) rank `r` of `n` candidates is priced `prior / (1 + ahead + r * gate / n)` under `relative` instead of `prior / (1 + ahead + r)`, with `n` the candidate count before the beam (the beam only cuts) and `ahead` a trait seed's implementors (0 elsewhere, so the plan's `1 + r * gate / n` for an ordinary node). The `ahead` term sits outside the factor on purpose: `(ahead + r) * gate / n` would raise the remainder's top price when a sibling is added past the gate. This form equals `rank` exactly at `n = gate` (continuous at the gate for every node, within 12 % of today at n = gate + 1 for ahead 0) and flattens a wide fan-out: 80 callers price the 9th edge at `prior / 1.8`, not `prior / 9`. At or below the gate nothing changes. The gate factor is a constant until a measurement asks for another. The pricing stays out of the handle, like gate and beam: a resumed call prices under the profile it is given and the handle does not refuse a different pricing, so one walk can mix pricings across resumes, as it can mix beams (the stamp of each call names the profile it ran under). The `start` trace event names `pricing` only under `relative`, so default traces are byte-identical. | K24b commit |
| d439 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | tests | Load-flaky timing tests fixed in the tests, never by throttling the machine and without weakening what a test proves (N88 closed: a test fault, the product caps the stage at its budget). Rules applied: a request timeout a test does not exercise is `LOADED_BUDGET_SECONDS` (the four fake-server fixtures of `navigation.rs` default to it, not 1 s; the idle, release, restart, loading-error and position-retry tests lost their 2 s and 5 s overrides); a test about a timeout keeps a short bound but gives the fake something that really outlasts it (`timeout` mode: 5 s against an `--initialize-delay-ms` of 30 s, asserting the product's "timed out after 5 seconds"; the stalled-write test waits for the fake's start marker before `initialize` and asserts the product's reason text, with elapsed in the message); a window that can lapse is made wide and aged by backdating instead of slept through (`models.rs` breaker tests: 30 s window, `age_past_the_window`); budgets are derived from `LOADED_BUDGET` (`lsp.live` version probe via `readiness_timeout_seconds` / `open_with_probe_timeout` in the shapes tests; git_history pre-merged build `per_node` 30 s; the t0 lexical map 15 s budget with real ripgrep; process-end waits 30 s). Stuck-span test: 5 s limit with a result that arrives 4 s late (new fake flag `--symbol-ok-delay-ms`), so the margin for a single call is 3 s instead of 1 s while the reset on a result is still the only thing that keeps the session. Progress-paced test: 8 s timeout, three 3 s gaps after the priming request (new flags `--progress-count`, `--progress-gap-ms`, `--progress-skip-priming`). The fake records `exit` before its other disk writes. N88's two tests: 15 s priming against 6 s ceilings, and 12 s budget against 4 s symbol queries (the start allowance was 3.5 s). Product finding, not changed here: N103 (`initialize` runs under the per-request `lsp_timeout_seconds`). Checked with a 20 to 30 process CPU burner beside the suites (scratch), not four cargo builds; `a_server_that_stops_reading_is_replaced_once` (its restart's `initialize` is bounded by the 2 s write bound) and `history_stops_at_the_head_the_map_was_opened_at` (30 s git timeout) still fail under the heaviest burner (26 to 30 processes) and are left. Also fixed on the way: `answers_to_cancelled_requests_do_not_make_a_server_stuck` (warm-up call before the 300 ms calls) and the discard holder test (12 s timeout). | fix/load-flaky-tests commit; suite counts in its message |

| d441 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | eval | K3a-2: the search evaluation records a language server past its search budget as data and warms the servers before the first query. `SearchReport` gains `lsp_over_budget` (the providers that missed `lsp_search_budget_ms`; absent from the JSON when empty, so a search without a budget hit serializes as before; the note stays). `QueryMetrics` gains `lsp_budget_hit` (always written, an old report reads false) and `lsp_providers_over_budget` (skipped when empty); `AggregateReport` gains `lsp_budget_hit_queries` and `priming` (workspace key, provider, `{primed, ms, error}`; skipped when empty). `lci_core::lsp::Manager::prime(workspace, timeout)` starts every enabled adapter whose version probe passes and waits for its priming, bounded by the timeout in total, an error per provider instead of a panic (decision B: every enabled adapter, not only the languages the workspace holds, so rust-analyzer's missing binary is a recorded failure, not skipped); `App::prime_language_servers` wraps it. `evaluate` primes each workspace key once before its first query when `[channels] lsp` is on; `RunOptions.prime` defaults to true and `evaluate --no-prime` keeps the cold first query (the user-facing flag, hence this row). `compare` and `evaluate --baseline` leave out a pair in which either side has `lsp_budget_hit` and count it (`budget_hit_pairs`, `_baseline`, `_candidate`, printed on the pairs line); `--keep-failed`, the name `maps compare` uses, keeps them. No run-profile key, no config key, golden unchanged; priming starts only adapters the configuration enables and is an evaluation-harness default, not a service default. Per-tag `lsp_budget_hit` counts (decision C) are a follow-up, not in this slice. A report written before this slice has no `lsp_budget_hit` (it reads false) and its first query per workspace was cold, so `compare` of an old report against a new one still pairs that cold first query. | K3a-2 commit |
| d440 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | freshness | K38: `[run_profile.freshness] stat_gate`, default `false` (every behaviour a leaf whose default keeps today's; the reviewed profile golden gains the one line `run_profile.freshness.stat_gate = false`). With `true` the read of the tree that `map_freshness` makes per traverse call (`freshness::tree_view`) and that a caller's search makes for `verify_on_read` goes through one lci-core function, `chunk::source_hashes`, on K8a's lister (`source_walk`/`source_entry`): a process-wide `StatGate` per root (canonical path, a call holds its root's gate for the walk and no other root's) keeps `(modified, len) -> hash` per listed file, reads and hashes only files whose stamp is new or changed, keeps "skipped" for a non-source `.ts` catalog, never gates a file with no modified time, and drops the entry of a file that is no longer listed. The per-file read, skip, decode and hash lines of the index scan moved into one `read_source` that both paths call, so a hash is the same function either way; with the key off the hashes are the same bytes through it, and the parse of changed files (whose chunks `tree_view` and `verify_on_read` threw away) is gone on both paths. The index scan (`scan_incremental_with`) still reads every file, the fingerprint and the manifest are untouched. `not_newer` takes the lister's modified time instead of a second stat per file. Known gap, documented in the README, the example config and the profile doc and asserted by `the_stat_gate_misses_a_same_length_same_mtime_edit_and_says_so`: a same-length edit that keeps a modified time older than 3 seconds before the previous read of the file (a deliberate `set_modified`, or a clock that went back) is not seen until the file's next edit. A racy-stamp guard (`RACY_WINDOW`, 3 s) closes the rest: a file whose modified time is within 3 seconds of the read that hashes it (or in its future) is not trusted, its entry keeps no stamp and the next call reads it again, which covers a same-tick write and the 2 s stamps of FAT and exFAT, SMB shares and 1 s filesystems (`a_stamp_near_now_is_racy_and_a_same_length_rewrite_is_seen`; the measured counts below are for files older than the window); the labels and `file_changed` are otherwise equal to the full-hash path's (`with_the_stat_gate_on_the_labels_equal_the_full_hash_path`, `the_lag_is_the_same_with_the_stat_gate_on_and_off`, `gated_hashes_equal_the_scan_hashes`). Measured (the speed proxy, files read per call, no wall-time claim; Windows, 2026-10-21, `source_hashes_with` with a counting reader): a 40-file fixture reads 40 files on every call with the key off; on, 40 on the first call, 0 on the second, 1 after one file's modified time moved. This repository (254 source files by the index's walk): 254 per call off; on, 254, 0, 1. Wall time of the call (one run, noisy, a cold first call): 828 ms off (first call), then on 917 ms first, 127 ms with nothing changed, 116 ms after one touch (what is left is the walk and the stats, not timed separately). Default on is a later slice with `timings.verify_ms` numbers and the GPT-6 Sol review that a default change gets (K13 waits for it). | K38 commit |
| d445 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | process | S3: `docs/decisions.md` is merged with `merge=union` (`.gitattributes`), so two PRs that each append a row no longer conflict on the tail; `cargo xtask docs check` (in `cargo xtask test --suite Full` and CI) now fails on conflict markers (`<<<<<<< `, `>>>>>>> `, a bare `=======`) in any tracked Markdown file and on a duplicate decision id or a row whose first cell is not `dNNN` in the log (ids may be out of order). Risk: a union merge of an edited existing row against an append keeps both versions of the row; the duplicate-id check catches that. GitHub's web merge ignores attributes, so only local merges and rebases get the union behaviour. | S3 commit |
| d444 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | traverse | K33a-1: trajectory events for evaluation runs, behind `lci traverse --trajectory` only (coordinator decision A: a CLI-only `Dirs.trajectory_candidates: usize`, 0 everywhere else; no `[traverse]` or `[run_profile]` key, so no golden line, no profile-hash change, no MCP or REST schema change, and nothing over MCP or REST can switch it on). With the flag, each counted pop writes one `pop` event just before its `expand` event: `{ev, pop, free, node, priority, depth, via, parent (the parent state's node or null), frontier (queue length after the pop), discovered, tokens_left, calls_left, candidates: [{node, priority, depth, via}..]}`, the candidates being the best `TRAJECTORY_CANDIDATES` = 8 queued states (decision B: the choice is made at the top of the queue) after dropping entries already expanded or cleared, best first. A pop that is put back (cut, or dropped for the budget) keeps its `pop` event, and its retry writes another one in the next call's trace, like its second `expand` event; K33a-2 counts pops from `end.totals` and marks such a row `counted: false`. Cost when on: one sort of the live frontier per pop; when off: one integer test. Size: about 1.2 KB per event with 100-character ids, so at most about 240 KB more per trace at the 200-pop cap. Off, every trace, answer, checkpoint and handle is byte-identical (the existing `equal_runs_write_byte_identical_traces_and_handles` still passes; a new test shows that, with the switch on, the trace without its `pop` events equals the plain trace and the stop hashes, totals, handle and snapshot are equal). On, the answer's `trace.hash` and `trace.events` move (`items.json` carries neither). The handle does not record the switch: a resume is given the flag again. `maps bench --trajectory` only passes the flag to the CLI (`CliTraverser.trajectory`, not in `RunConfig`, so no report file changes). The unknown-gold label is `null`, never `false` (decision D) and the join is a separate command (decision C): K33a-2 (`maps trajectories`, the join of these events to the gold outcome) follows. | K33a-1 commit |
| d442 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | lsp | K10a-1: `[run_profile.lsp] places`, default `"scip"` (today: the SCIP table alone, no place for a symbol the index lacks, so no request and no edge under `scip.mode = off`, d411). `"outline"` layers `OutlinePlaces` over it: the first time a walk expands a symbol the index places in a file, or a file or chunk, `lsp.live` asks the server's `textDocument/documentSymbol` once per file and map instance (one counted external call; a failed request keeps the index's place and is not asked again) and, where a file is learned, the outline's places win over the index's (the `LostEdge` gap of `lsp.live` closes under `outline` (`PLACED_BY_OUTLINE`); it stays labelled `SiteDrift` because card sites are the index's). A member takes the index's id for the same file and name (nearest line; a function or method of either kind), else a minted `SymbolKey` with the file's real language (`sym:csharp:Shapes.cs::Area/method`, planned decision B; the SCIP ids keep `rust`, a known wart of `default_node_ids`), equal names numbered in document order. Kinds with a node: LSP 12 function, 6 and 9 method, 5 and 23 struct, 11 trait (decision D: class and interface map to `struct` and `trait` now, K10b re-maps), 10 enum, 2-4 module, 14 const; fields, properties and variables get none until K10b. The outline range of a nested `DocumentSymbol` now runs from its name to the item's end, so `Member::end_line` is the item's last line, and `ScipPlaces::defined_in` reads the name's own line before the range. "symdefs" is the extractor, not the store (decision A): the bundle store `sym.defs@1` is filled only by `bundle::sync` from the index manifest, which a traverse call does not hold, and K10a-2 will join Rust members to `chunk::extract_for_path` declarations. A Symbol seed whose file was never outlined still has no place (decision C: module-path inversion goes with K21a). The key is in the run-profile golden (re-blessed: the new key line and the hash) and the recipe stamp. | K10a-1 commit |
| d447 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | eval | `maps bench --config <toml>`: one configuration overlay for every `lci traverse` call of a run, so a bench arm can switch a `[run_profile]` key (K21a-2 seed arms, K24c ceilings: fusion `log_odds` against `boost`, `[run_profile.walk] pricing` `relative` against `rank`, `[run_profile.seeds] bm25`). Mechanism: the CLI's own global `--config` (the same file format and loader as every other command, `LCI_CONFIG` stays the fallback when the flag is absent), passed before the subcommand to every call of `CliTraverser` (the item calls, the default-maps probe and the map preflights); `CliTraverser.config` is `None` everywhere else (`sim`), so nothing changes there. Run identity: the file is read and hashed before any other work (a missing file stops the run, before the gold is read), and its SHA-256 enters `RunConfig.overlay`: the row's note gains `config=<file stem>@<first 12 hex of the hash>` (no path, so a committed trend stays machine-neutral) and `score.json` gains `config_overlay {path, sha256, profile_hashes}`, the last being the distinct `profile_hash` values the traversals reported in their raw output (the effective run profile; the CLI already stamps it on every answer), so two runs with different overlays never read as one. `--rescore` accepts the flag for the identity only (no traversal runs). Without `--config` no argument, note, row or file changes (tested: the command line, the note parts, and `run.json`, `items.json` and `score.json` equal to a run with an overlay minus the one added block). The overlay replaces the config the CLI would otherwise find, so it must carry the data directory and service ports the run needs. | K21a-2 and K24c prerequisite |
| d443 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | eval | K5a-1: `cargo xtask maps baseline rg --set traits` or `--set where-to` (with `--gold-dir`, `--tree`, `--out-dir`; optional `--top-k 10`, `--wave`, `--limit`, `--rg`, `--decl-first`, `--max-per-file 50`), an xtask-only baseline arm beside the impact and history arms (no profile key, no config field, no golden line, default change none). Per item: needles from the question only (the last segment of each backticked name, at least 2 chars, deduped, at most 4; none backticked: `lexical::terms` cut to 4, matched case-insensitively and counted apart in `meta.json`; neither: no answer), one `rg --json --fixed-strings --word-regexp --max-count` run over the frozen tree with the language adapters' globs, answers `{path, line}`. Order: traits by distinct needles hit desc, then path, then line, one answer per line; where-to one answer per file (best line by the same order), files by matched lines desc, then max needles, then path; `--decl-first` puts lines with a declaration keyword before the first needle ahead (wave `baseline-rg` is rg as typed, `baseline-rg-decl` is rg as an agent reads it; both are baselines, neither tuned on the gold). Own rg runner in xtask rather than waiting for K22a-1's `search_literals` (the command line is the same; a later XS can swap it). `expansions` and `model_calls` are 0; the K3a-1 cost keys are absent, so `maps compare` pairs none on them. d422 (10)/(11) hold: a baseline beside the seed arms, never a gain claim. Three rules, named per wave in `meta.json["rule"]`: `fixed` (the default above, wave `baseline-rg`), `fixed-decl-first` (`--decl-first`, wave `baseline-rg-decl`) and `regex` (`--rule regex`, traits only, wave `baseline-rg-regex`), which reproduces the earlier `rg_arm.py` driver behind d392's rg row: one `rg --sort path --glob *.rs` call per question with an impl-header regex and a derive regex built from the trait name the question gives (Q1/Q2/Q3 templates read from the question text), Q1 one answer per matched line (cut to `--max-answers 40`), Q3 the first `fn member` at brace depth 1 of each impl block, Q2 as Q3 with headers naming the type after `for` first (cut to `--top-k`); the fixed rules do not pair with d392, the regex rule does: whole-word fixed-string needles hit every mention of a name, not its impl headers, so on traits the fixed rules score far lower (pooled Q1+Q3 set_f1 0.15-0.34 on tauri, bevy, deno against 0.66-0.75 for regex) and would flatter the seed arms. rg has no total-output cap or timeout beyond the per-file `--max-count` (out of scope here; a later slice may add one). The LSP-over-MCP arm is K5a-2, after K11d-1's harness. | K5a-1 commit |
| d446 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | lsp | K7b-3a: readiness signals per language server, as a report with no key and no change to a request, repeat or answer. The transport keeps the `is_ready` latch as it was (the adapter's `ready_predicate` matched once, never cleared) and gains a `watch` of `ServerState { busy, changes, changed_at }` fed by a new adapter method `busy_predicate` (default: never matches, so six adapters are untouched; rust-analyzer: `experimental/serverStatus` with `quiescent == false`; jdtls: `language/status` `Starting`, added in `java.rs` at the coordinator's request; jdtls never waits for ready, so its session reads `busy` between `initialize` and `Started`): a ready match clears `busy`, a busy match sets it, each matched notification bumps `changes`. `JsonRpcClient::spawn` keeps its signature (it calls the new `spawn_with_busy` with a predicate that never matches), so no existing caller or test changed. `Manager::readiness(provider, workspace_id)` returns `Option<ServerReadiness { state: starting\|ready\|busy\|failed\|stuck, primed, progress, ready_changes, age_ms, changed_ms, idle_ms }>` (`None` with no session; it does not touch `last_used`, as `running`). Read by `Status.analyzers` (new field of `index_status`, one entry per adapter, `null` when the workspace has no session for it; `analyzers_running` unchanged), by the search over-budget note (appends "Server state: csharp-ls: starting for 2 s" or "rust-analyzer: busy for 3 s, 2 ready changes"; the first sentence is unchanged) and by `lsp.live` (one note per provider whose state is not `ready` when the walk ends; a ready server adds nothing). Fake server: `--status-cycle` (ready, then busy after `initialized`; ready again after the answer to the next request) and `--status-shape rust`. Tests: `a_busy_signal_flips_the_state_and_counts_the_changes`, `manager_readiness_reports_starting_then_ready_and_the_status_shows_it` (starting under the fake's own `--initialize-delay-ms`, ready, `failed` for a missing binary, `null` for the six others), `a_busy_signal_makes_the_session_report_busy_and_then_ready_again` (the session reads `busy` with two changes while the fake holds the priming answer, then `ready` with three), the rust and jdtls predicate units, `the_server_state_word_follows_the_precedence` (the stuck state is a unit of the precedence function, since a stuck session is replaced by the call that notices it), `the_readiness_clause_names_the_state_the_duration_and_the_changes`, and extensions of the mcp status check and the cold-server note check; none sleeps on a timer. K7b-3b (`[run_profile.lsp] busy_repeats`, which would make a busy rust-analyzer's "file not found" transient again) is not built: the sanity-8 knob freeze defers every new key until the K24c ceilings run, and K11d-1 measures the N8 dark windows with this report first. | K7b-3a commit |
| d438 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | measure | K11d-1: `cargo xtask maps lsp-cost` (xtask/src/maps/lsp_cost.rs, questions xtask/questions/lsp-cost.json) runs `serve` on 127.0.0.1:8769 per arm over MCP `search_code mode=traverse` with `lsp.live` added through `[traverse.policies.explore] extra_maps` (an MCP call has no `maps` argument; `steer.policy` takes only the five preset names, so a policy named `lsp-cost` is refused), and reads `totals.per_map["lsp.live"]`; the one product line is a `debug!` of the version probe's ms (`lsp.live version probe`). Measured on this tree (HEAD 8e6fd44, debug build, rust-analyzer 1.98.0, vectorless index, 12 questions, 3 rounds quiet and 2 loaded, deadline 20 s, 24 logical cores; the "quiet" condition shared the machine with other lanes and the "loaded" one (12 busy CPU threads) with a foreign job on top, both recorded as idle CPU in the report; medians, ms per `lsp.live` expansion): `per_call` (the default) starts a rust-analyzer every call: in the quiet condition 34 of 36 calls ended on the 20 s deadline with 5 expansions each, warm and cold alike (p50 6.7 s per expansion, 1.3 pops per 5 s); the loaded `base` arm is bimodal (8 of 24 calls finished before the deadline at about 10.5 s with 14 to 200 expansions and a 0.5 GB rust-analyzer, 16 ran to it; cause not established), so its row mixes two groups; `sessions = shared`: cold call 17.6 s for 3 expansions (5.9 s per expansion), then p50 185 ms and p95 1.0 s per expansion, 30 expansions per call, 17.5 pops per 5 s, 6 of 36 calls on the deadline, and 37 ms for a question asked again; loaded: p50 240 ms, 26 expansions per call, 13.7 pops per 5 s. `outline_cache` changes nothing for rust-analyzer (it is not resent file text; p50 198 ms, no hit possible) and stays for pyright, gopls, jdtls, clangd; `implementations` showed no visible rise in external calls per expansion (1.63 against 1.74 for `shared`; the walks differ) and p50 114 ms; `sync = watch` cost nothing readable: `shared+sync` p50 84 ms, `per_call+sync` equal to `base` (p50 6.6 s), RSS unchanged; the all-on `lifecycle` arm p50 77 ms, 41 expansions per call, 32.6 pops per 5 s. RAM: rust-analyzer peaks at about 4.0 GB in every arm and the service at 260 to 310 MB. Probe (N101): `rust-analyzer --version` alone 99 ms p50 quiet, 2.6 s p50 and 4.3 s max under the synthetic load; in the service the probe ran 5.04 s (timed out, `lsp.live is unavailable`, 6 calls) in one quiet arm while another lane was working, 3.4 s in another; so the 5 s default fails under ordinary lane activity. N8: in the `shared` arm 6 of the first 30 calls ended on the deadline (7 loaded), never more than 3 in a row, and the same questions (q01, q04, q06, q09 to q11) were slow again in round 2 (14 to 18 s), so the dark calls follow slow expansions of particular symbols (priming) rather than a time window; the analyzer state at each dark call needs K7b-3a. Meaning for the default-off keys: `sessions = shared` is the only key with a large effect (about 36 times lower ms per expansion warm, 13 times the pops per 5 s) and the walk is not usable without it under the 5 s default deadline, since one cold start costs 17 s; it should be on wherever `lsp.live` is on (K11b), with a deadline above the cold start; `outline_cache` and `sync` cost nothing visible and gain nothing for rust-analyzer, so they stay off until a server that needs them (K11d-2) shows a gain; `implementations` shows no measured cost here and waits for the trait-set recall measurement; `call_hierarchy` (merged after the measured tree) is not measured: the first follow-up is an arm `shared+calls` against `shared` after merging main; K11b must also fix N101 (probe default or the probe outside the walk deadline) before `lsp.live` is on by default. Not measured: release-build timings, other servers (K11d-2), the analyzer state per dark call. | K11d-1 commit |
| d448 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | traverse | K22a-1: `[run_profile.seeds] literal`, default `false` (one golden line, `run_profile.seeds.literal = false`; the key joins the profile hash and reaches the recipes, whose seeds are given). The map `seeds.literal` is an exact-literal seed source over ripgrep and is not `[traverse] exact_seeds` (that turns the first stage's SCIP-closure and pickaxe cards into start nodes; nothing ran `rg` for the names a question backticks, d397, d422 (5)). Coordinator decisions: A the names `seeds.literal` / `literal` (what it matches, not the tool); B the runner is injected through `SeedSearch::literal_search` (default `None`; `AppSeeds` runs `lexical::search_literals` over the resolved workspace with `ripgrep_path`; a stub in tests, no fake rg on Windows; without a runner the call gets the note "seeds.literal needs the application's ripgrep" and goes on, fail-open); C its nodes are appended after the hybrid and `seeds.bm25` nodes, not prepended (`seed_source` `hybrid+literal`, `hybrid+bm25+literal`, `bm25+literal`, `literal`; the name-token stand-in runs only when nothing answered); D declaration-shaped lines give a `file:` seed, which is what the seedless-trait case (d394) has instead of no seed. Needles: the last segment of each path of `code_names(question)`, at least 2 characters, at most 4, once each. Search: `rg --json --line-number --fixed-strings --word-regexp --sort path --max-count 20` (case-sensitive; a line is kept only when it holds a needle as a whole identifier, `_` and alphanumerics on neither side, checked again in code, and a unit test runs the real ripgrep to pin that `--word-regexp` treats `_` as a word character: `Shape_kind` and `my_Shape` do not match `Shape`). Placing, per line: `SymbolPlaces::defined_in` (a `definition` seed), else `enclosing` (an `occurrence` seed, counted per node), else a `file` seed when the word right before the name is a declaration keyword (`fn trait struct enum impl class interface type mod def func union record protocol`) or the name follows `for` of an `impl .. for` and the line is no comment, else dropped (a name in a signature or constant is a use). The places are the SCIP layer (`Loaded::places()`, built once per index stamp on first use; K10a's outline places can replace it with one argument later). Order: definitions (needle order, 3 per needle at most), occurrences (most lines first), files (by path); nodes the other sources gave are left out and the list is cut at `seed_k`. Reuse (sanity check 8): `lexical.rs` now has the one ripgrep runner (`run_ripgrep`) and the one JSON line parser (`parse_matches`, a closure decides which lines are kept) that `search` and `search_literals` share; the K5a xtask arm is to be pointed at `parse_matches` later, not given a second parser. Not measured: whether the seeds help (K22a-2 owns the depth-0 bench and the seedless-trait mechanism test); `map_freshness` does not label the map (freshness.rs is outside this slice); the `lci traverse --maps` help text in main.rs does not list it (follow-ups: a freshness label for `seeds.literal` in `freshness.rs`, and the `--maps` help text); a file seed needs a source file (prose files such as `*.md` give none), and `declares` also reads `function`, `object`, `delegate` and a Go receiver `func (r T) Name`; the real-ripgrep unit test that pins `_` as a word character skips itself where `rg` is not installed. | K22a-1 commit |
| d449 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | lsp | K10a-2: under `lsp.places = "outline"` a Rust member the index has no node for takes the id of the declaration `sym.defs` would store, not a minted one. `OutlinePlaces::with_tree(root)` (the traverse call passes `scip_root`) makes `learn` of a `.rs` path read the file from disk (the bytes the index hashes; a document the editor holds open may differ), run `chunk::extract_for_path`, and give a member the canonical id (`CrateNames::read_paths(root, &[path])`, new in `lci-maps/src/crates.rs`: the same `Cargo.toml` lookup as `read`, for the listed Rust files only) of the innermost declaration of the same name whose one-based `start_line..=end_line` holds the member's line and that no earlier member took (a trait's `m` and an impl's `m` stay two nodes), the kind and container from the declaration. The order is the index's node (same file, name, nearest line), then the declaration, then a minted id (no declaration, an unreadable or unparsable file, no tree, or a declaration id the index places elsewhere). So a walk seeded by `sym.defs` ids and this map name one node. Known limits: `read_paths` sees only the file's own crate, so a package name that another unlisted crate also uses, or that is another crate's directory, is not dropped as `read` drops it; a minted Rust id keeps the directory form of the module path (not canonicalised). Placing a Rust Symbol seed whose file was never outlined stays with K21a (decision C). No new key: the run-profile golden is unchanged. | K10a-2 commit |
| d451 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | eval | K33a-2: `cargo xtask maps trajectories --runs <bench out dir>.. --gold-dir <dir> [--scip <index>] [--set <name>]` (xtask/src/maps/trajectories.rs; a separate command, decision C, so no file of a bench run changes) joins the `pop` events of a `maps bench --trajectory` run (d444) to the gold outcome. Per item of each `<wave>/<set>` it reads the raw output, the trace and the item's `items.json` record and writes one row per `pop` event to `<wave>/<set>/trajectories.jsonl` (overwritten per run; a stale file is removed when the run has no pop events) and a summary `trajectories.md` beside it (items, items without pop events by id, pops, gold choices, pops with a gold candidate skipped, median first gold pop). A row is one serde `Row`: `{set, wave, item, trace, event, pop, free, counted, state{depth, frontier, discovered, tokens_left, calls_left}, choice{node, priority, via, parent, gold}, candidates[{node, priority, depth, via, gold}], expand{offered, adopted, cut, dropped}\|null, outcome{gold_candidate_skipped, gold_before, item{f0.5, headline metrics}}}`; `SCHEMA` lists the keys and a test checks each. Labels come from `is_gold` only (the set's own scorer on the answer the bench adapter offers for the node; the site from the raw output's cards, else the SCIP index with `--scip`); a symbol node no card or index located is `null`, never `false` (decision D), and `gold_candidate_skipped` is `null` when a candidate is gold and the choice is unlabelled. Pops are counted from `end.totals` (`pops` plus `pass_through_pops`; counted rows when a trace has no `end`); with an `end`, the first `end_pops` pop events are `counted` and the rest (in practice the last one: the bench never resumes) are `counted: false` (the K33a-1 review: a budget put-back pop event can exceed `end.totals.pops` by one and the next call reuses its number; but a put-back whose call timed out stays in `end.totals.pops`, lci-maps traverse `cut.then_some(adopted > 0 || timed_out)`, so the rule is the totals, not the `expand` event, which decides only for a trace with no `end`: `dropped`, or `cut` with `adopted` 0). Such a row is written and is left out of the summary and of `gold_before`. `gold_candidate_skipped` is `null` when a candidate is gold and the choice is unlabelled, or when no candidate is gold and one is unlabelled; `false` only when every candidate is labelled and none is gold, or the choice is gold. The engine writes `"pop": totals.pops` for a free pop too (`pass_through` repeats the previous paid number), so the summary's first gold pop is the row's position among the item's `pop` events, and rows are ordered by `event`. Size: a row is at most about 2.3 KB with 100-character ids and 8 candidates (asserted in a test; the plan said about 1.4 KB, which left out the candidates labels and the item metrics each row carries), so rows = pops, a 100-item set at the 200-pop cap at most about 46 MB, typically a few MB, under `test-results/`. No profile key, no golden line, no change to a trace, answer or bench file. | K33a-2 commit |
| d450 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | lsp | N101: DEFAULT CHANGE, `readiness_timeout_seconds` 5 to 15 s (range 1 to 30 unchanged), and the `lsp.live` `PROBE_TIMEOUT` of `ManagerBackend::open` with it (the traversal already passed the setting; `open` kept its own 5 s). Evidence (d438): standalone `rust-analyzer --version` p50 99 ms quiet, p50 2.6 s and max 4.3 s under the synthetic load; in the service the probe timed out at 5.04 s in one arm (6 calls, `lsp.live is unavailable`, while another lane worked) and took 3.4 s in another; a failed probe is not cached, so each later call paid its own 5 s. 15 s is 3.5 times the loaded maximum and equals `CALL_TIMEOUT`. Contract change, not only an lsp.live one: the one key also bounds `/ready`, `init`'s survey and MCP `service_status`, so they now wait up to 15 s for a hanging dependency, and a dependency that answers in 5 to 15 s is now reported ready where it used to time out; a refused port or a missing binary still fails at once. A separate `lsp.live`-only key was not added (new keys are frozen). The version probes of a traversal now run side by side under one bound (`ManagerBackend::open_with_probe_timeout`), so two hanging binaries cost one bound, not two. It is in `EXCLUDED_KEYS`, so no run-profile hash moves. No code change for the deadline: the probe already runs inside the setup window the walk credits back to its deadline (`budget.deadline += setup.elapsed()`), so only the bound mattered. The first traverse after a warm start can run two `--version` probes (the warm task's and the walk's, both before the first success is cached); one server starts, and the cost is accepted. New: a first-touch warm start, `App::warm_language_servers` (background `Manager::prime`, once per workspace and process, never awaited by a request), called from `status` only when the workspace has a snapshot and no build runs, and from `search` after the index is resolved and no build runs (not from `index`, which would compete with the build, nor from navigation, which starts the server itself); it starts only the adapters whose language the snapshot holds (`Manager::prime_languages`, beside the unchanged `prime`). The check for a running build and the spawn happen under a read guard of the workspace lock; residual risks: a build that starts while a prime is already running competes with it, as a search during a build does; and a build may replace the snapshot between the language read and the guard (the check needs an await, which the guard must not be held across, and the snapshot carries only `indexed_at`), so the warm start can use the language set of the previous snapshot, which at worst leaves an adapter for a newly added language to the first walk's cold start or primes one the new index no longer needs. `release_language_servers` and shutdown abort and await the workspace's warm task before releasing (every overlapping stop caller waits on the same end signal; the task is never taken out of its entry), so no session appears after a release (the released workspace is not warmed again; a release that finds no warm start leaves a tombstone under the same lock, so a `status` racing the release starts none, and shutdown stops any later one). It runs only under `[run_profile.lsp] sessions = "shared"` with `traverse.enabled` and a `[traverse.policies.*]` `extra_maps` naming `lsp.live`, so it is inert under today's `per_call` default; `App::warm_start(id)` exposes the outcome (tests) and each provider logs one `lsp.live warm start` line. Test fixtures take `readiness_timeout_seconds = LOADED_BUDGET_SECONDS` in `fake_csharp_fixture` and the `with_fake_*` helpers, and the two direct `ManagerBackend::open` tests use `open_with_probe_timeout(.., LOADED_BUDGET)`. `xtask maps lsp-cost` no longer pins 5 s, so its next run measures the default. Re-measuring `lsp-cost --arms shared` under lane load is a separate step after merge (its probe ms goes into this row). The PR needs the cross-family review (default-changing slice). | fix/n101-probe-bound |
| d454 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | lsp | SHARED: DEFAULT CHANGE, `[run_profile.lsp] sessions` `per_call` to `shared`, with per-adapter routing and a tree barrier so that no answer is staler than under `per_call` for any server (the golden `run_profile_default.txt` moves by the `sessions` line and the hash `fa3c7da5...` (main with K13) to `c17ca88e...`, nothing else). Evidence (d438, rust-analyzer 1.98.0): warm p50 185 ms against 6,666 ms per `lsp.live` expansion (about 36x), 17.5 against 1.3 pops per 5 s, and `per_call` could not finish a walk under the 20 s deadline (34 of 36 calls). The rule (coordinator): the default may not make an answer staler than `per_call` would. Routing: `LspAdapter::watches_workspace() -> bool` (default `false`, no config key) is `true` for rust-analyzer alone (its client capabilities declare no `workspace.didChangeWatchedFiles`, so it runs its own file watcher); pyright, gopls, jdtls and clangd are resent only the requested file, csharp-ls and typescript-language-server nothing, so they do not qualify. Round 4 (d460, after the Sol re-check): a server is shared only if it also declares a non-empty `LspAdapter::stamp_manifests` list (`LspAdapter::keeps_session(synced)`), because an edit to a configuration file it reads (`tsconfig.json`) moves neither a source file nor a directory time; jdtls lists none and so stays per call even under `sync = "watch"` (csharp-ls got a list too, `*.sln`, `*.csproj`, `Directory.Build.*` and so on, so the fake-server tests keep their shared server; the coordinator's list named five adapters). `ManagerBackend::open_split(workspace, shared, own, probe)` probes through `shared` and routes each probed server to `shared` when `adapter.keeps_session(shared.sync_mode() == Watch)`, else to `own`, the call's per-call manager (a server there starts on the tree as it is and stops with the walk); `open` and `open_with_probe_timeout` (one manager) are unchanged for the CLI and every other caller. The walk-end readiness notes read each routed (server, manager) pair (`ManagerBackend::routes()`). The N101 warm start (d450) primes only the languages whose adapters the walk would share. The barrier (round 3; the first idea, a stat walk by the index rules, was dropped: deno 1.52 s p50): a kept session is not trusted to see an edit, whether through the server's own watcher (unbounded lag on large workspaces, bursts, new files) or `sync = "watch"` (N102: the Windows watcher goes silent on overflow). `Manager::tree_barrier(workspace, adapters)` runs once per walk, in `open_split`, before any shared server is started or reused: it takes a tree stamp per adapter (`lsp::stamp`) and compares it with the one recorded when that session last started or passed; a different stamp, or a session with no recorded stamp (started by a call that passed no barrier), stops the session, so the walk starts a fresh one that reads the disk as `per_call` would, and the walk adds the note `lsp.live: <server> restarted: the tree changed since its last call`; an equal stamp keeps it. The stamp is built from the index file list and one directory read per stamped directory, with no recursive walk (round 4 widened it): a stat of the adapter indexed files; of every directory holding one and all its ancestors up to the root (a file added, removed or renamed there moves the directory mtime), with the directory's child names hashed too; of one level of child directories of those (mtime), except `.git`, `target`, `node_modules`, `obj`, `bin`, `build`, `dist`, `out`, `__pycache__`, `.venv` and `venv` (build output and version control, whose mtimes move with every build or git command), so a file created in an existing unindexed `src/generated/`, which a `#[path]` attribute can name (Sol new-1, reproduced first: the stamp did not move), is seen; and of the adapter configuration files among those children, matched by name or one-star pattern in every stamped directory (`LspAdapter::stamp_manifests`: rust-analyzer `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`, `rust-analyzer.toml`; typescript `tsconfig.json`, `jsconfig.json`, `package.json`, `tsconfig.*.json`; pyright `pyrightconfig.json`, `pyproject.toml`, `setup.cfg`; gopls `go.mod`, `go.sum`, `go.work`; clangd `compile_commands.json`, `compile_flags.txt`, `.clangd`, `CMakeLists.txt`; csharp-ls solution and project files and their property files), as counts plus an order-independent hash of (path, mtime, length), on up to 8 threads. Only `NotFound` counts as absent; any other metadata or directory-read error fails the stamp, and the barrier restarts the session and records nothing (Sol new-3; the earlier `.ok()` turned every error into an absent file or a zero time). Child directories and configuration files are stat'd by path, not taken from the directory listing: on Windows `DirEntry::metadata` is the listing's cached copy, which lagged a change in the first version of this widening (found by the unit tests). The barriers of one workspace run one at a time (a per-workspace async lock held across stamp, compare, stop and record; Sol race at `lsp.rs:1130`, confirmed by reading: two overlapping barriers could record an older stamp over a newer one and stop a server a later walk had just started; test `concurrent_barriers_of_one_workspace_run_one_at_a_time`). The file list is the workspace manifest paths (`App` implements `stamp::IndexedFiles` over `manifest::try_load`, kept per manifest stat, so an unchanged index costs one stat); a manager with no source, or a workspace with no snapshot, has no stamp and so no sharing (every walk restarts). The warm start records its stamp before it primes. Only the traverse `lsp.live` path passes the barrier: navigation (`symbols`, `definition`, `references`) and the search channel reuse the service sessions as before this slice (their pre-existing staleness is its own item, N104); a session they started has no stamp, so the first walk restarts it once. Stamp cost (local, release build, warm cache, p50 of 5, file list given): deno 46 ms (1,142 stat'd files and 214 directories; single-threaded 186 ms; a walk of the same tree is 1,520 ms), bevy 96 ms (2,042 files, 441 directories), tauri 25 ms (356, 148); round 4, with the directory reads and one level of children (same trees, release build, p50 of 7, file list given): deno 68 ms (min 58, max 83), bevy 109 ms (94 to 113), tauri 64 ms (61 to 69; under 512 tasks it runs on one thread), all well under the 300 ms kill rule; the cold read of an 86 MB manifest of a 6,003-file repository is 569 ms once per index change, a 7 MB one 43 ms. The rule, restated in round 4 (an absolute "never staler than `per_call`" would need a full content walk, which failed its kill rule at 1.52 s on deno): a reused session is never staler than `per_call` for any change inside the stamp's coverage (the indexed files, their directories and one level of child directories, the ancestors, the configuration files); outside it the residuals are listed in the README and `config.example.toml`, and the answer carries a label: the walk adds the note `lsp.live: <server> kept session; tree stamp unchanged since <n> s ago` for every session the walk found running (not for one it started, nor one the barrier restarted), so it is distinguishable from a fresh `per_call` answer (no schema change). Round 5 (d463, Sol re-check 2): the first version took `kept` from the barrier, which missed a server started by an overlapping walk that passed its barrier at the same time (confirmed: both barriers pass before either starts a server, the second then reuses it through `Manager::session` with no note). It is now decided where the session is acquired: `Manager::session` records, into the `WalkSessions` of the walk that made the request (a `tokio::task_local!` set by `ManagerBackend` around each request, so the warm start, navigation and the search channel are not recorded), whether it started the session or found it running, with the age of its recorded tree stamp; the traversal reads `ManagerBackend::walk().reused()` when the walk ends and writes one note per reused provider (`... kept session; no tree stamp was recorded for it` when the stamp is missing); `tree_barrier` again returns only the restarted providers. Also round 5: the README and architecture text that said `observed = "live"`, `stale = false`, `lag = 0` meant `lsp.live` read current bytes now says `live` names the channel, a server this call started reads the disk, and a kept server is bounded by the stamp's coverage and carries the note; the emitted metadata is unchanged. Test: `a_walk_that_finds_a_server_an_overlapping_walk_started_is_labelled_kept`. Round 6 (d465, Sol re-check 3, new-1 resolved, new-2 partly): README and the freshness module comment still said `lsp.live` reads current bytes (now: queries a server; a fresh one reads the disk, a kept one is bounded by the stamp's coverage), and `answer_labels` gave an `lsp.live` card `fresh` at lag 0. Decision: a card citing `lsp.live` is `unverified`, not `fresh`, when the walk found a server running (`WalkSessions::reused()` non-empty, passed to `add_answer_labels` as `kept: &[MapId]` = `[lsp.live]`); a card from a server this walk started keeps `fresh`; a `changed` or `stale` finding stands. The rule is per walk, not per provider or card (a conservative simplification: with several servers, one reused server makes every `lsp.live` card `unverified`). No schema change, no golden or snapshot file moves (the key defaults off, and the word set is unchanged). Round 7 (d466, Sol re-check 4): a checkpoint keeps the cards of a walk that reused a server, and a continuation whose own walk makes no kept-session request has an empty `kept`, so restored cards could read `fresh`. Chosen: demote, with no checkpoint format change and no provenance flag: `add_answer_labels` takes `restored` (the node ids of the snapshot's states, read before the traversal resumes) and a restored card that cites `lsp.live` is `unverified`; cards of nodes the continuation's own walk reaches follow `kept`. Conservative: a restored card from a walk that started its own server is demoted too. Tests: `restored_lsp_live_cards_stay_unverified_in_a_continuation` (unit) and `a_continuation_keeps_the_restored_lsp_live_cards_unverified` (navigation: a one-pop walk over a kept server gives a handle; the continuation, with no shared manager, keeps those nodes `unverified`). Round 8 (d467, Sol re-check 5): `Manager::document_symbols` answered a cached outline (`outline_cache`) without recording the session in `WalkSessions`, so a walk served only from the cache had no note and `fresh` cards. `Manager::live_session_id`, which the cache lookup goes through, now records the running session (`Manager::note_walk`, shared with `Manager::session`) before the cached outline is returned; the first record per session id stands, so a session the walk itself started (recorded as started by its earlier request) stays its own. Audit of the other paths that answer from a session or a cache: the only cache is `Manager::outlines` (`document_symbols`); every request method (`symbols`, `definition`, `references`, `implementation`, call hierarchy, the uncached outline) reaches `Manager::session` through `client()`; `probe_once` caches a version probe, not an answer; `readiness`, `running`, `synced`, the idle sweeper and `release` read or end sessions without answering; `lsp_live.rs`'s `places.learn` is per call (`OutlinePlaces` is built for each walk). Test: `a_walk_served_a_cached_outline_by_a_kept_session_is_labelled_kept`. Round 7 also qualified the `lsp_live.rs` module doc and the `edge.rs` `observed` and `lag` field docs. Round 6 tests: `a_card_citing_a_kept_session_map_is_unverified_not_fresh` (unit, `card_freshness`) and `a_card_from_a_kept_session_is_unverified_and_one_from_a_fresh_session_is_fresh` (navigation, `map_freshness` and `answer_labels` on, fake csharp-ls). Residuals, by construction: a new file two or more levels below a stamped directory (or inside a skipped build-output directory) and in no directory that holds an indexed file; a configuration file that is not on the adapter list; an edit that keeps both mtime and length; and an edit during the call, which `per_call` has too. The server watcher and the sync are no longer relied on for correctness; `sync = "watch"` no longer avoids a restart after an edit for `lsp.live` (forwarding the changed paths to a kept server, `didChangeWatchedFiles` plus a quiescence wait, is a later optimization to be measured on its own). The `lsp.live` label stays `Live` (a label change alters answers under `map_freshness`, K13/K7c). Rejected: replacing a non-watching session when the snapshot generation or a freshness stamp moves (under `freshness = manual` the stamp never moves); resending text to csharp-ls and typescript before each request (K7c lifecycle work, and typescript refuses a second `didOpen`); a per-call `source_hashes` scan (K38 gate, d440). Local smoke, real rust-analyzer 1.98.0, not CI (`run/shared-smoke/result.md`), default profile: no edit between two walks, the server (pid 35932, warm-started in 12 s) is reused by both (walk 195 ms and 119 ms, no restart note); control (SCIP index of the edited tree, live tree unedited): no restart and `run_twice` not reached (`Invalid offset LineCol { line: 16 }`), so the answer comes from the server view; then a new caller of `add` was appended: the next walk stopped the server and started a new one (pid 32268, the old one gone), noted `rust-analyzer restarted: the tree changed since its last call`, took 9.1 s (a cold start of a one-file crate inside the walk, under the 20 s deadline) and reached `run_twice` through `lsp.live`. The first-round smoke (barrier absent) had shown the rust-analyzer watcher delivering the edit within 0.11 s, one observation that does not bound its lag, which is why the barrier exists. Resource effects: a rust-analyzer a walk keeps stays (about 4 GB on a large crate, d438), one per workspace touched, until `lsp_idle_timeout_seconds` (1800; `0` forever; a priming or serving server is never released), `release_workspace`, replacement (`lsp_stuck_seconds`) or shutdown; every edit costs the next walk one cold start, as under `per_call`, and every unchanged walk costs the stamp; the N101 warm start is live for configs with `traverse.enabled` and a policy naming `lsp.live` (a stock config starts nothing). Off switch: `sessions = "per_call"` (moves the hash); the idle timeout only limits residency. The CLI one-shot costs the same as before. `xtask maps lsp-cost` arms `base` and `per_call+sync` now write `sessions = "per_call"`, so d438 rows stay comparable. Tests: `only_rust_analyzer_watches_its_workspace` (real adapter table), `only_servers_with_a_stamped_configuration_list_keep_a_session`, `concurrent_barriers_of_one_workspace_run_one_at_a_time`, the round-4 stamp units (`a_new_file_in_an_unindexed_child_directory_of_a_stamped_one_changes_the_stamp`, `a_configuration_file_matched_by_name_in_a_stamped_directory_counts`, `name_patterns_have_at_most_one_star_and_ignore_case`, `only_not_found_counts_as_absent_any_other_stat_error_fails_the_stamp`), in navigation `a_new_file_in_an_unindexed_child_directory_restarts_the_shared_server`, `a_configuration_file_edit_restarts_the_shared_server`, `a_stamp_that_cannot_be_taken_restarts_the_shared_server_every_walk` and the kept-note assertions of the kept and restarted tests, `open_split_routes_by_watching_and_sync`, the `lsp::stamp` units (an edit, a new file beside an indexed one, a manifest edit and a removed file each change the stamp; other languages, unlisted files and unrelated directories do not; order independence), `lsp_sessions_defaults_to_shared_and_per_call_changes_the_profile_hash`, and in navigation (fake csharp-ls under `sync = "watch"`, which makes it shared; `watching_sessions_fixture`, `indexed_manager` with a fixed index snapshot, `age_tree` so a directory mtime cannot share a tick): `a_shared_server_is_kept_while_the_tree_does_not_change` (spawn 1, no note), `a_shared_server_is_restarted_after_an_edit_between_two_walks` (the former K8a test `a_shared_server_is_told_about_an_edit_between_two_walks`, now spawn 2, the note, the new caller found), `a_new_file_in_an_indexed_directory_restarts_the_shared_server`, `a_change_in_an_ignored_directory_keeps_the_shared_server`, `without_an_index_snapshot_a_shared_server_is_not_kept`, and under the default profile with `sync = "off"` `the_default_profile_sees_an_edit_on_a_server_that_does_not_watch` and `the_default_profile_keeps_nothing_in_the_service_manager_for_a_non_watching_server`; the retry helper retries only a clean deadline miss and fails on any other error note. Re-measuring `lsp-cost --arms base,shared` (real rust-analyzer, the d438 index) is a coordinator step after merge. The PR needs the cross-family review (default-changing slice). | feat/shared-sessions-default |

An edge therefore says the destination is a unit in a test file or a test module, not a `#[test]` function: the map can offer fixture helpers inside `mod tests` and misses a `#[test]` function outside a tests module; the ceiling run reports how many offered edges are not `#[test]` units. `role_of_decl` in `src/app/recipes.rs` keeps the older rule (`test` always counts) and is unchanged so no recipe answer moves; one shared rule beside `Chain` is the follow-up. Needs `--root` like the other tree maps (a note and `unavailable` without it); a note counts the test units found at build. The bench knows it (`map_emits`: `Symbol`, so `can_answer(Impact)` holds). Ceiling (`maps bench --set impact` with and without the map, `tests_recall`, `tests_deep_recall` per stratum) is measured separately and is a bound, not a gain claim over rg or LSP (d422 (11)); K32-2 `docs.mentions` and K32-3 (pickaxe as a walk map, a check) follow as their own rows. | K32-1 commit |

No locator, so edges go to `File` nodes only and `emits` is `[File]`; no `DocSection` node is made. Documentation means the role rule: any `.md` file wherever it sits and files under `doc`, `docs` or `documentation`; a root `config.example.toml` is `Source`, so the toml half of the config-key gold is out of reach by contract and `config.key` is the map for key to toml. Heading-section nodes are a possible follow-up (K32-2b, unplanned, cycle 1 optional). Decision G: the ceiling run is not part of this slice; like K32-1 it is open and runs in the measurement lane (`maps bench --set impact` with and without the map, `docs_recall` on the 30 config-key items, bounded near 0.5 by the gold's toml half; 54 of the 109 gold doc entries are `README.md` rows). Any number from it is a bound, not a gain claim (d422 (11)). The `--maps` help in `src/main.rs` names the map.
