| d470 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | lsp | ASYNC-1: the three small fixes of the async audit, no default, key or answer change. (a) `lsp.live` expansion: the cap `CALL_TIMEOUT` (15 s, `min`ed with the budget's deadline) is gone; an expansion is bounded by the walk's own `deadline_ms`, and each request inside it by the transport's inactivity bound (`lsp_timeout_seconds`). (c) The version probe is memoised for the process (`probe_subprocess_version`: key resolved path, modification time and size; the arguments are always `--version`; success only, a failure is never remembered; a binary whose file cannot be read, such as a bare name on a platform without lookup, is probed every time, so a bare name is not memoised there; `/ready` and `service_status` stay live: `src/app/readiness.rs` runs its probes (ripgrep and every adapter) inside `adapter::fresh_probes`, a task-local scope that bypasses the memo and records nothing, so a broken binary is reported at once, test `fresh_probes_run_again_after_a_memoised_success`; only LCI's own probe paths, `Manager::probe_once` and the walk's, keep the memo); `Manager.probed` stays as the per-manager fast path and the memo for stub adapters. The first traverse after a warm start no longer runs two probes, and `cargo test -j` spawns the fake's `--version` once per binary, not once per `Manager`. d450's remark that `base` probes per call and `shared` once is retired for `xtask maps lsp-cost`: the probe is per process, so every arm's later calls log about 0 ms (comment and Method text updated). Tests (no sleep, no wall-clock assert): `an_expansion_waits_as_long_as_its_budget_allows` (paused clock, fails with the 15 s cap), `an_unbounded_warm_up_waits_for_a_slow_probe` and `a_warm_up_with_a_hanging_probe_ends_only_by_abort` (a gated probe, Notify and Semaphore), `hanging_probes_share_the_one_timeout_of_a_bounded_prime` (paused clock, exact end), `a_binary_is_probed_once_per_process_until_its_identity_changes` (a counting closure); the deadline test of `lsp.live` loses its `elapsed` assert. `test-util` is a dev feature of tokio in `lci-core` and `lci-maps` only. Two of the three 2026-10-21 wall-clock flakes: `a_refused_embedding_connection_skips_queries_and_lets_index_runs_probe_once` (breaker window 600 s for the test, `Models` counts requests, the probe must add exactly one; the cause was plausible, not captured) and the `grep.live` correctness tests (every test builds its map with a 3600 s scan timeout and budget, `docs.mentions` tests through a test-only `DocsMentions::patient`, no product timeout or default changes; cause confirmed: the default 3 s scan timeout stops a scan per file under load and gives a short edge list). The third, `the_live_lexical_map_passes_c1_and_c6_and_fails_open_without_ripgrep` (real ripgrep under a 15 s budget and a 25 s wall assert), needs a scanner seam in `channel_maps.rs` and goes to ASYNC-2. Not fixed here and left to ASYNC-2: `didOpen` only on changed text and a per-URI diagnostics wait for the 3 s `head_start`, readiness by event, the other wall-clock test asserts, polls to waits. | commit of ASYNC-1 |
| d471 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | traverse | K20b-1: `[run_profile.scip] mode` defaults to `cache` (it was `authoritative`; `authoritative` stays as the strict opt-in, `off` is unchanged). A readable index gives byte-identical answers and the same handle identity, since no code path reads the mode after the load. A missing or unreadable index gives an answer over an empty SCIP graph with the K20a note instead of an error. Under `cache` a file that fails to parse is parsed once per file stamp (size and modified time, the graph cache stamp): `app::traverse::index` keeps one remembered failure per (index, root), replaced when the stamp changes, removed when the file loads (also on a graph cache hit), never read or written under `authoritative` or `off`, and the call says "not re-read" while it is remembered. Round 2 (d472, review findings): only a parse failure on bytes that were read is remembered (an I/O error or a stat error is retried by the next call); a remembered failure expires after 30 s (a constant, no key, no golden change; the note says "after 30 s"), so a repair of the same length within one modified-time tick is read again; the failure is inserted only if a re-stat shows the stamp that was parsed. Recipes with no index refuse with the hint to run `rust-analyzer scip` or set `traverse.scip_index`. The golden `run_profile_default.txt` moved by the `scip.mode` line and the hash line only; no key or leaf was added; the profile hash of every result stamped under an unset mode moves, and K11d-1 numbers (d438) carry the old hash. No `role` label and no arbitration: both wait for K20c, since no authority runs before K9b and K11b. | K20b-1 commit, tests `an_unreadable_index_under_the_default_mode_is_parsed_once_per_stamp`, `a_default_server_answers_without_or_over_a_corrupt_scip_index` |
| d474 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | lsp | K11e (first commit of the K11e + N103 PR): an opt-in LSP down-window breaker, `[run_profile.lsp] breaker = false` (bool, in the profile hash; the golden `run_profile_default.txt` moved by the `run_profile.lsp.breaker = false` line and the hash line only). Under `true`, `Manager` holds `lsp::LspBreakers`: one 30 s window per provider (`LSP_DOWN_WINDOW`, the `ConnectBreaker` of the model clients, now `pub(crate)`), shared by `Arc` between the service's manager and every call's own manager (`per_call_manager` hands a call's manager the windows of `Call.lsp_sessions`, under both `sessions` values; a call with no shared manager has none). What opens a window: a failed `probe_once` (the version probe, including its own timeout), and a server start (`Session::begin`, once per start whatever the number of waiters) that failed for any reason except the transport's inactivity timeout: a spawn failure, an exit, a protocol break, `initialize_params` or `after_initialized` failing (decision B of the plan: a binary that crashes at start is a dead server, and a start has no restart-once path). What does not: a timed-out start, since `transport::InactivityTimeout` (a typed root error whose text is byte-identical to the old message) tells a slow `initialize` from a dead server (the coordinator note and N103); a request failure or an `RpcError`; a started server, which is never refused (the window is read only where a probe or a new start would run). What a window does: `probe_once` (before its memo's miss) and `Manager::session` (a new start only; now `Result`) refuse the provider with "skipped for N s after a failed probe or start (lsp breaker)" until the window passes or a probe or start succeeds (`clear`); the walk's `lsp.live is unavailable` note then carries that text. `/ready` probes the adapters directly under `fresh_probes` and never reads a window. Default off, so every answer, note, trace and report is byte-identical to before; the only change under the defaults is the profile hash for the new leaf. Turning it on by default is K11b. Tests (no sleep, no wall assert; the `expire` test hook ages a window past its end): `a_failed_probe_opens_the_window_and_the_next_caller_is_refused_without_a_probe`, `a_failed_start_opens_the_window_and_the_next_request_is_refused_at_once`, `without_the_breaker_every_request_starts_the_server_again`, `a_timed_out_start_opens_no_window` (lci-core `lsp`), `lsp_live_skips_a_dead_server_for_the_window_and_a_live_one_closes_it` (the walk with the fake, windows shared across two managers, key off and on), the existing `fake_csharp_lsp_timeout_and_malformed_protocol_return_bounded_errors` timeout arm run with `breaker = true` (no window), `lsp_breaker_is_off_by_default_and_changes_the_profile_hash`, and the recipes test with a third profile `breaker: true`. N103 (`lsp_start_timeout_seconds`) is the PR's second commit and is not decided here. | commit of K11e |

Plumbing: `Manager.start_timeout` (the key, or `lsp_timeout_seconds` when 0) goes through `Session::begin` and `GenericClient::spawn` to `JsonRpcClient::request_within("initialize", .., start_timeout)`; `request` is `request_within(.., self.timeout)`. Only `initialize` uses it: requests to a started server, `initialized`, the `after_initialized` hooks and the adapters' `TransportConfig.timeout` are unchanged, and the inactivity text is unchanged (it names the bound that applied). A timed-out start is still a transport failure that is not retried, and opens no breaker window. Decisions A (a key, default 0) and C (the fake's 8 s `initialize` delay is the one wall wait) of d474. Tests: `a_slow_initialize_is_bounded_by_the_start_timeout_not_the_request_timeout` (the fake, `lsp_timeout_seconds = 5`, delay 8 s, `lsp_start_timeout_seconds = LOADED_BUDGET_SECONDS`: `symbols` answers, one spawn; it fails when `initialize` ignores the start bound), `lsp_start_timeout_seconds_outside_its_range_is_rejected` (86401 rejected, 0 accepted), the machine-keys profile test now sets the key to a non-default value and the hash does not move. | commit of N103 |
| d475 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | lsp | K37b: concurrent walks as the load test of the tree-stamp barrier (SHARED, d454), with the fake language server; no product change, no default or key change. Four findings, all clean. (1) No stale reuse within the stamp's coverage (indexed files of the server's languages, their directories and ancestors, manifests; an edit outside it is the documented residual, not a finding): after an edit between walks two overlapping walks both answer the new tree (`Twice` and `Run`), with a fake that ignores the sync's `didChangeWatchedFiles` (new fake flag `--ignore-watched-files`, test support only, off by default, no existing test's events change), so the barrier's restart is the only way the edit reaches the kept server; a walk opened before the edit gets the new tree from its next request after another walk's barrier stopped its server. (2) Restarts are serialised per workspace: one restart and two servers in all for two overlapping walks over an edit (`spawns/` counts 2, never 3: the two walks share the post-edit start), and the unit test now proves the per-workspace lock without a timer (a stub that holds every stamp until the test hands it a permit; three barriers, never more than one stamp in flight). (3) The notes are right: two overlapping walks over a cold manager share one start and exactly one is labelled "kept session", none "restarted"; over an edit exactly one answer carries "restarted" and at most one "kept session"; a walk whose own second server started after a stop has no kept session in its record. A walk that restarted the server may also be labelled kept when the other walk's request began the new server first; this was not observed in the runs and is allowed by the assert. (4) No per-server request queue: two walks sharing one server process (a definition and a references request) each got their own answer, with no cross-talk and no extra start; the per-id matching itself is by construction (the transport's id-keyed pending table, d422) and is not tested against an out-of-order answer, since the fake answers in arrival order; N20's ordering clause (the part K37a was meant to absorb) is closed with this row as evidence. For servers that open documents, two walks' `didOpen` of one file carry the same disk bytes, so the interleaving is harmless there too (reasoned, not tested; the fake logs no message ids, so the test checks that both requests reached one process and that each answer is the right one). The optional real rust-analyzer smoke was not run (it does not gate the merge, d470); a smoke failure would invoke the SHARED kill rule. Tests (no sleep, no `elapsed`, no wall-clock assert, spawns counted with `spawns/`): `two_overlapping_walks_share_one_server_and_exactly_one_is_labelled_kept`, `an_edit_between_overlapping_walks_restarts_the_server_once_and_both_answer_the_new_tree`, `a_walk_whose_server_the_barrier_stopped_answers_its_next_request_from_the_new_tree`, `interleaved_requests_of_two_walks_on_one_server_each_get_their_own_answer` (navigation) and `concurrent_barriers_of_one_workspace_are_serialised_without_a_timer` (lci-core, replaces the sleep-based `concurrent_barriers_of_one_workspace_run_one_at_a_time`). | K37b commit |
| d476 | 2026-10-21 | DECISION | process | Development-speed review (owner asked; approved "Proceed"). Measured over 12 h of cargo-slots.log: all 4 build slots busy 9 % of the time, 0 to 1 builds 37 %; free RAM never below 20 GB, VRAM at most 2.2 GB; disk queue about 0.5 since Full gates run one at a time; CI about 9 to 10 min per run. So the machine is not the bottleneck and the slots stay as they are; the critical path is waiting on unmerged slices, shared doc lines that conflict across PRs, review rounds and serial measurement arms. Changes: (1) only docs PRs and the coordinator rewrite the roadmap's "Now and next" line; (2) a slice that depends on an unmerged one starts on a branch stacked on it and merges `origin/main` once the base lands; (3) a serial library-test pass (`--test-threads=1`) runs only when a diff adds process-wide state: measured 216 s serial vs 62 s parallel for all library tests (warm target), over the 2 min bar for every push; the trigger was the ASYNC-1 probe memo making `the_version_probe_honours_the_timeout_it_is_given` order-dependent on CI; (4) measurement arms that use no model service and are judged by counts may run side by side on their own frozen copies and data directories, with wall-clock latency from a separate sequential sample (evaluation-workflow exception); (5) a jobs 8 vs 16 spike for a lone Full gate follows, kept only if faster with disk queue at most 3 |
| d479 | 2026-10-22 | DECISION | lsp | ASYNC-2 commit A (item 1, step 1): a position request sends `textDocument/didOpen` only when the file's text changed since the session sent it. The session (`GenericClient`) owns `SentTexts`, a uri to content-hash table (`workspace::hash`, the hash the outline cache uses); `open_if_changed` reads the file (16 MiB cap as before), answers `None` for an unchanged or oversized text, else sends `didOpen` v1 and answers the uri; a failed send forgets the entry. `before_position_request` gains `sent: &SentTexts`; the five adapters (python, typescript, go, clangd, java) use the helper. A new session starts empty, so a restarted server is re-sent its files. In this commit the 3 s `head_start` sleep of python, typescript, go and clangd stays but only behind a sent text, so a repeat request on an unchanged file costs no `didOpen` and no sleep; java never slept. TypeScript still re-sends a changed text and the server refuses it as before (not synced until K7c's `didChange`). The hook is serialised per client during an open: `SentTexts` also holds one gate per uri, taken by `open_if_changed` before the comparison and kept by the returned `OpenedDocument` until the hook ends (head start included), so a concurrent request for the same file waits for the send in flight (the `didOpen` write is awaited to the end, so it is on the pipe first) and neither overtakes the text nor skips the head start; a request for another file, or for an unchanged file with no open in flight, never waits. The table and the gates hold one entry per navigated file for the session's life. No key, no default value, no answer change; the outline cache's premise (the server holds the current disk text) is kept, since an edit changes the hash and is sent. Commits C (readiness by event, injected clock) and D (polls to waits) follow; the Sol review of d477 covers A to C. COMMIT B (the per-URI `publishDiagnostics` wait replaces the sleep; stacked on A): the transport counts `textDocument/publishDiagnostics` per document URI (`Diagnostics { by_uri, notify, wait_timeouts }`, noted by the reader for any notification with a `params.uri` and its `version`; the table is keyed by a canonical form of the uri, see below), and `JsonRpcClient` offers `diagnostics_seen(uri)`, `wait_diagnostics(uri, seen_before, version, cap)` and `diagnostics_wait_timeouts()`. The count a wait compares against is read INSIDE `open_if_changed`, after the uri's gate is taken and just before the `didOpen` is written, and kept in the `OpenedDocument` (`wait_for_diagnostics(rpc, cap)`), so a publication that follows the write at once (the fake's default) cannot be counted into the reading and leave the wait to run to its cap. The hook still holds the document (and so the uri's gate) until the wait ends, so a concurrent request for the file waits for the publication too. python, typescript, go and clangd wait for `min(lsp_timeout_seconds, MAX_DIAGNOSTICS_WAIT = 3 s)` (d477 answer A set the configured bound alone; the coordinator capped it at the old 3 s worst case after the review, because a server whose uris never match would otherwise pay the whole configured bound on every open; the cap is to be lifted once K11d-2 shows `diagnostics_wait_timeouts == 0` on the real servers); the sleep and its "head start" comments are gone; java still waits for nothing. The table key is canonical (`canonical_uri`): a `file` uri is percent-decoded and its drive letter lower-cased, so our `file:///C:/w/a.py` (from `Url::from_file_path`) and the `file:///c%3A/w/a.py` that `vscode-uri` servers (pyright, typescript-language-server) publish are one document; any other uri is its own key. Real-server smoke (2026-10-22, debug binary, `lci definition` on a two-file tree, `[python] path` / `[typescript] path`, index vectorless): pyright published `file:///c%3A/...` for the opened file (our uri was `file:///C:/...`), 1 to 2.4 s AFTER a 3 s cap on this machine in a cold per-call session, so with the cap every python call still ends its wait at the cap (the same worst case as the old sleep; with the cap raised to 30 s in an experimental build the wait ended on the publication and no warning appeared, which also proves the canonical match end to end); typescript-language-server published nothing at all because the client did not declare `textDocument.publishDiagnostics`, so the typescript `initialize` now declares it, and with it the publication arrived (experimental build: the call took 3.6 s against 5.8 to 8 s). gopls and clangd were not run (not installed here). The 3 s cap therefore saves nothing yet for pyright on a cold start; lifting it is for K11d-2. (The smoke ran with the latch and the warn log; both are gone, the observations stand.) There is NO per-client latch (the Sol review of A+B showed one: a wait that ran out is not proof that the server never publishes, and pyright publishes after the cap when it starts cold, so a latch would have left every later file of the session without a wait): each open waits up to its cap, which is the old worst case per open; a wait that runs out is counted in `wait_timeouts` and logged at debug. Version correlation (best effort): each `didOpen` of a document carries `version` 1 for the first text sent in the session and one more for each text after it (recorded in `SentTexts` with the hash), the reader keeps the `version` of the latest publication of a uri, and a wait is not answered by a publication that carries a version below its own; a server that publishes no version, or a late publication of the previous text with the same version, can still end a wait early, so it is a signal, not proof that the server holds the current text. Open and cancellation (Sol finding 2, in A's code): the hash is recorded only after the `didOpen` write succeeded, in the detached write task itself (`JsonRpcClient::notify_holding`), and that task also owns the uri's gate until the write ends, so a caller cancelled mid-write neither leaves a text taken for sent that never reached the server (a failed write after a cancel used to leave the hash behind for good) nor frees the gate while the write is still going on; a failed write records nothing (the old text stays recorded, which is what the server holds). Open gap (Sol re-check 1, PLAUSIBLE, no real-call repro): a caller cancelled mid-write whose write then succeeds records the text with nobody waiting for its diagnostics, so the next caller skips the open and may be answered before the server finished analysing the file; roadmap row N111 (keep a pending wait for the next caller). The caller's own deadline (the walk's, d470) cancels the future. The fake publishes empty diagnostics for every `didOpen` in every mode after `--diagnostics-delay-ms` (default 0, never with `--no-diagnostics`) and records `diagnostics_sent.txt`; with a delay it reads on a thread, and a document request it reads while a publication is still due is recorded as `request_during_pending_diagnostics.txt` (a client that waits for the publication never causes one). No key, no default value, no fake answer change; behaviour change named for the review of d477: a request on pyright, tsserver, gopls or clangd waits for the server's diagnostics of the opened file (bounded by `lsp_timeout_seconds`) instead of 3 s, and the real-server effect is measured by lsp-cost and K11d-2, not assumed. Test timing (item 5) for the files B touches: lci-maps `the_deadline_is_never_exceeded` (failed once under 9-lane load, d493) no longer sleeps or reads the wall: the map's second expansion never answers (a gate, `hangs_from(2)`), only the walk's 2 s deadline can end it, and the outer `timeout(60 s)` is the only clock; a paused tokio clock was not usable because the walk compares a `std::time::Instant` deadline and only its timeout rides the tokio clock. | tests `sent_texts_report_a_change_once_per_new_hash` (unit), `a_request_for_a_file_being_opened_waits_for_the_open_to_finish` (new file `tests/cases/lsp_concurrent_open.rs`, documents-mode fake, two concurrent requests on one client: the second parks until the first hook ends; fails when the gate is removed), `a_second_request_on_an_unchanged_file_sends_no_second_did_open` (fake typescript and python, `did_open_seen.txt` counts 1, 1, 2, 2; fails with 2 when the hash check is removed); commit B: `a_diagnostics_wait_ends_on_its_uri_not_another`, `a_publication_is_matched_to_its_file_whatever_the_spelling`, `a_publication_for_an_older_version_does_not_end_the_wait` and `every_wait_that_reaches_its_cap_is_counted_and_waits_in_full` (transport unit, paused clock), `sent_texts_are_current_only_once_recorded` (adapter unit, replaces `sent_texts_report_a_change_once_per_new_hash`), `a_cancelled_open_whose_write_fails_is_not_taken_for_sent` (`lsp_concurrent_open`, a server that stops reading and a 4 MiB text; fails when the hash is recorded before the write), `lsp_diagnostics_wait::` (new file `tests/cases/lsp_diagnostics_wait.rs`: `the_request_follows_the_opened_files_diagnostics_not_a_timer`, `a_second_request_for_the_file_waits_out_the_diagnostics_wait`, `a_publication_that_beats_the_hook_back_from_the_write_still_ends_its_wait`, `a_server_that_never_publishes_costs_each_open_at_most_the_cap`; counters and the fake's state files, no sleep, no elapsed assert), lci-maps `the_deadline_is_never_exceeded` converted to a gated map; no process-wide state added |

| d483 | 2026-10-22 | DECISION | engine | K19 built: `grep.structural`, an opt-in tree map (`[run_profile.grep] structural`, default false; one golden line `run_profile.grep.structural = false` and the hash line moved, nothing else). Mechanism: `rg --fixed-strings --word-regexp` names the files that hold a symbol's name (at most 200, path order), each file up to 1 MiB is parsed once by the chunker's own tree-sitter grammar and searched with ast-grep patterns, so a name in a comment or a string gives no edge. A header match (`impl .. for ..`, `extends`, `implements`, `class C(Base)`, `class C : Base`, a C++ base clause) gives an `Implements` edge from the seed to the other party of the header (its symbol when the index declares it in the same file, else the file; confidence 70), a call match a `CallsOrRefs` edge to the enclosing function (confidence 55); witness Exact, label `Tags`, freshness Live, needs `rg`; Go has call patterns only (interfaces are structural). Key semantics as `seeds.bm25`: off gives a note and no edges and the call is byte-identical to one without the name; on, the map is in the default set of a call with `--root` that names no maps. Dependency: `ast-grep-core =0.40.1` (MIT; `default-features = false, features = ["tree-sitter"]`), the last release on tree-sitter 0.25, so `Cargo.lock` keeps one `tree-sitter` (0.25.10) and no grammar crate moved; it adds `bit-set` 0.8.0 and `bit-vec` 0.8.0 (Apache-2.0 or MIT); `ast-grep-language` is not used because it carries its own grammar crates. It moves with K15a (0.40.5 or 0.44.1 for tree-sitter 0.26; 0.45 and later need 0.27). Findings that shaped the pattern table: a metavariable `$X` needs an identifier character in the pattern (ast-grep's expando, `µ` here, with `pre_process_pattern` replacing `$` in the pattern only, the searched source is never rewritten); a pattern that stands alone parses differently from the same code in a body (Go `s.Draw(x)` is a type conversion at the top of a file; C `draw(x)` a declaration; a C++ base clause has no class), so those entries are contextual (a larger code text and the kind of the node that is the pattern); `{ $$$ }` stands for a body where the grammar needs one. Not seen: derive and macro impls, Go and duck-typed headers, Rust headers with a `where` clause, a generic seed type (`impl Shape for Wrapper<T>` is not found from `Wrapper`), C and C++ member calls (`a.f()`, `p->f()`) and calls inside a Rust macro's token tree; a bare trait name stands for every trait of that name (`Display`). The map states a live freshness like `grep.live` (the live and placed-by-index lists of the freshness labels), and `maps bench` turns `[run_profile.grep] structural` on in that arm's derived overlay when `--maps` names the map (as for the seeds keys, d461). No ceiling number yet (waves `k19-ctl`, `k19-on`, `k19-only` on the traits set are open; none is a gain claim, d422 (11)). | K19 commits, tests `a_rust_impl_header_pattern_finds_the_implementing_types_and_not_other_traits`, `a_call_pattern_finds_call_sites_and_not_comments_strings_or_definitions`, `every_pattern_of_the_table_compiles_against_its_grammar`, `grep_structural_is_off_by_default_and_is_part_of_the_profile`, `naming_grep_structural_with_the_key_off_gives_the_note_and_no_edges` |
| d480 | 2026-10-22 | DECISION | lsp | ASYNC-2 commit D (item 4 of the async audit; lci-maps and `src/app/mod.rs` only, no key, default or answer change): four polls become waits. (a) The data-directory index lock (`lock_data_dir_for_indexing`): a plain `std::thread` takes the OS lock, blocking in `lock()`, and the caller awaits a `oneshot`; `INDEX_LOCK_RETRY` (100 ms) is gone, so the holder's release ends the wait. A dropped caller ends its wait at once, and the thread unlocks and ends when it gets the lock (its send finds no receiver); a plain thread is not joined by the runtime's shutdown, which was the reason for the old poll, so a stopped `serve` still exits at once. (b) `lci_maps::process::wait_limited(child, limit, KillScope) -> Waited { status, timed_out, reaped }` (a waiter thread owns the child, the caller `recv_timeout`s on a channel; the kill is by pid, whole tree for `probe_map::run_limited` as before, the process alone for `exact_history::run_capture` as before, then the waiter reaps with a 2 s grace; the waiter keeps the `Child` until the caller has finished the kill step, so on Windows the open handle keeps the pid from being reused while `taskkill` resolves it, and a failed waiter spawn kills the child at once; on Unix `wait()` reaps, so there the pid is reserved only until the child exits) replaces the 5 ms and 10 ms `try_wait` loops. (c) `probe_map` `wait_for_gate` (`GATE_POLL` 5 ms) becomes `Gate { busy, Condvar }` with `wait_timeout` until the walk's deadline and a release that notifies; a probe still gives up at its deadline. Tests (no sleep, no elapsed assert): the lock wait is proven with a callback that fires on the waiting thread just before it blocks, then the release; the gate wait with a sleep counter that shows the waiter parked before the release; the child tests assert `timed_out`, `status` and `reaped`. Not changed, by contract or boundary: the index watcher poll, the transport and model-service bounds, `lsp_search_budget_ms`, the sync poll fallback. No process-wide state added, so no `--test-threads=1` pass is owed |

| d488 | 2026-10-22 | FINDING | engine | N105: `lci traverse --maps seeds.hybrid` died in a debug build on Windows with `thread 'main' has overflowed its stack` (exit 0xc00000fd) right after "indexing without vectors". Cause: not recursion but the size of the debug build's futures. Windows gives the main thread 1 MiB, the CLI's `#[tokio::main]` polled `run()` there, and a traverse call that creates the index inline through `seeds.hybrid` (hybrid search, index creation without an embedder, the walk) peaks at about 1.0 to 1.25 MiB of stack on the polling thread. Fix (DECISION, no default, key or answer change): `fn main` now spawns a thread named `main` with a 16 MiB stack (`MAIN_STACK_BYTES`; only address space is reserved, pages commit on use) that builds the same multi-thread runtime `#[tokio::main]` built (`new_multi_thread().enable_all()`) and `block_on`s the old async main; the thread is joined and a panic is resumed with `resume_unwind`, so the panic message, the 101 exit code, the error-return path and the `process::exit` calls are unchanged; signal handling runs inside the runtime as before. Why a thread rather than boxing hops: boxing moves one future's state to the heap (d0d10e4 boxed the traverse call in `run` for this very reason) and fixes one call path, while the next feature that adds a few hundred bytes to a debug frame on the polling thread overflows again; the limit belongs to the thread, not to the call, and a bigger stack for the thread that polls the root future covers every CLI command (the MCP and REST server run requests on 2 MiB tokio workers, about 0.75 MiB of headroom over the measured debug peak; no overflow known there). Evidence for the size: with a spawned-thread stack of 1.25 MiB and up the repro passes, at 1 MiB it fails; the triage found no recursion | regression test `indexing::traverse_seeds_hybrid_runs_through_the_binary_without_overflowing_its_stack` (real binary, tiny tree and SCIP file, embedder URL on closed port 9, empty data dir; fails with `#[tokio::main]` restored, passes with the thread); stack sweep in the N105 triage record (work area, not in this repository) |

| d485 | 2026-10-22 | DECISION | engine | K18a-1 built: `seeds.repo_map`, an opt-in start-node map under `[run_profile.seeds] repo_map` (default false; one golden line `run_profile.seeds.repo_map = false` and the hash line moved, nothing else). Its top `seed_k` nodes go after the hybrid and `seeds.bm25` ones (`seed_source` `hybrid+repo_map`, `hybrid+bm25+repo_map`, `bm25+repo_map`, `repo_map`, each also with `+literal`), each with a `seed_hits` record `how: repo_map`, `score_kind: pagerank`, `personalised` and `iterations`. Parameters (64, 0.85, 30) are Aider's and networkx's defaults, not fitted; with the 1e-9 tolerance most graphs run all 30 iterations (the error is about 0.85^30). Honest limits: only functions and methods are nodes (types, traits and modules are never seeds), the prior is as stale as `graph.scip`, and generic utilities (`new`, `fmt`) are hubs. A `Fetcher` trait with a test double keeps the tests off the network. LongCodeArena: the parquet files are converted by a one-off script outside the repository (pyarrow in a local venv under the gold directory; one JSON object per row with the parquet column names, and `changed_files`, which the dataset stores as a Python list literal in a string, written as a JSON array) to `<subset>.jsonl`; `convert --source lca-bugloc --in <dir>/lca-bugloc` reads every `<subset>.jsonl` present, tags each item with the sha256 of its parquet file from `fetch.json` (and fails when the parquet file on disk no longer matches `fetch.json`), and the report lists every file hash plus one `sha256` over the sorted parquet hashes. `convert` takes the revision from `fetch.json` unless `--revision` is given. Leak-rule fixes from the K4a review (these change d484's rule; items are classified by the new rule): the stem is split into tokens like the question (camelCase and `_`), so `UserService` in a question and `user_service.py` as a member match (a question word with the same letters once `_` is dropped, or the stem's tokens next to each other in plain words; a stem whose tokens are all generic never); the repository owner's tokens are ignored like the repository name's; the generic directory tokens gain `java`, `python`, `node`, `packages`, `common`. A test for each, and a mutation check (the stem split forced off, the owner left in, the five tokens removed: each makes exactly its test fail). Counts of the real run (2026-10-22; held out, recorded and not asserted; counts and hashes only, no question or path text; the gold stays in the owner's gold directory): SWE-PolyBench Verified, revision b3fca77b637379f0c01ad86d18753a7ac1998b53, `test.csv` 12,410,402 bytes, sha256 0c8138e73c34fa29a5276b675b146b72d78ce001fcc4560d76302c908b4808a5: 382 rows (the card says 394; the CSV holds 382 records, checked with a second reader), 382 items, none dropped, leak 298 and clean 84, truncated 10, python 113, javascript 100, typescript 100, java 69, 20 repositories, members per item p50 1, p90 4, max 34, excluded files: created 28, test 1, unsupported extension 49, overlap with the local gold trees 0. LongCodeArena bug localization, revision f2bb26a1c3320ccc1d7b02d2630077ff327a80f6, test split of each subset (py 175,518, java 249,815, kt 196,122 bytes; `repos.json` 5,002 bytes also fetched): 150 rows, 100 items, 50 dropped as `no_member` (the Kotlin rows, which have no LCI adapter), leak 85 and clean 15, truncated 4, python 50, java 50, 93 repositories, members per item p50 1, p90 5, max 14, excluded files: test 108, unsupported extension 124, created 0, overlap 0. Total download 13,036,859 bytes. Against the pre-registered expectation: PolyBench rows 382 (the card says 394) and items 382 are inside the 330 to 394 items range; the leak rate of 78 % (expected 20 to 50 %) and the LongCodeArena leak of 85 % are outside it, so ten items of each source were read by hand: the conversion is right (members are the changed non-test files, the questions are real issue texts), the high rate is real (bug reports name the class, the file or the function), and the rule was not tuned to move it; truncation 2.6 % and 4 % is under the 5 % bound; the Kotlin subset is dropped whole. Markdown members (`CHANGES.md`, `docs/.../*.md`) stay because Markdown has an adapter. Which rule fired (measurement added after the Fable review; what counts as a leak is unchanged, the leak and clean counts above are the same before and after): the check reports the first rule that fires, over all members, in the order path verbatim, stem as one word, stem under another spelling of its tokens, directory token. The item's stratum is `external/clean` or one of `external/leak-path`, `external/leak-stem` (word or tokens) and `external/leak-dir`; the where-to scorer's `leak` dimension is the part after `external/`, so a score report shows `leak-path`, `leak-stem`, `leak-dir` and `clean`, and the strata report counts every value but `clean`, `n/a` and `none` as a leak (a test pins both). The report holds `leak_rules` (path, stem_word, stem_tokens, dir_token; counts only). Split of the real run: SWE-PolyBench Verified 298 leaks = path 59, stem_word 144, stem_tokens 16, dir_token 79; LongCodeArena 85 leaks = path 22, stem_word 47, stem_tokens 4, dir_token 12. So the stem and path rules, which name the file itself, make 203 of 298 and 69 of 85; the directory-only rule is 79 and 12, and the K4a-review fix (stem tokens) fires on 16 and 4. Cycle 2 reads `leak-path`, `leak-stem`, `leak-dir` and `clean` as separate groups. Held out by contract, unchanged: nothing here is called from a tuning, preset or default path, and cycle 2 reads the set once, at the end, with leak and clean read separately. Check: `gold::external` 28 tests (12 new on K4a's 16: stem spelling, owner, generic directories, JSONL reader, LongCodeArena convert with hashes, fetch refusal and pinning (`fetch.json` unchanged after a refusal), fetch subsets, one test per leak rule, the strata per rule); xtask 496 tests green. The HTTP client is built inside the download thread's own runtime. `test-results/external-gold/report.json` is under the gitignored `test-results/`, so the counts live in this row and the PR body. |
| d501 | 2026-10-22 | DECISION | process | Decision rows no longer go in with the slice that makes them. Every merged PR dirtied every other open PR on GitHub through the tail of `docs/decisions.md` (the local `merge=union` driver resolves the tail, GitHub cannot), so each open PR paid one re-merge plus one CI round (about 15 to 20 min) per merge. Rule, in the manner of d476 (1) for the Now line: NEW slices do not append `decisions.md` rows; the implementer writes the row text into the slice's plan file, and the coordinator folds the rows into `main` in a docs-only PR after each batch of merges. Branches already in flight keep their rows (no churn). The union driver can duplicate a row that was edited on both sides, so every merge of a branch that touches `decisions.md` is checked for duplicate rows (a repeated `dNNN` id). Sanity check 13 kept this rule and considered moving the target into the repository as one file per PR (d505). |
| d503 | 2026-10-22 | FIX | lsp | N107: tokio's `Command::output()` pipes only stdout and stderr, so a child it starts inherits the parent's stdin, unlike std's `output()`, which uses a null stdin. `probe_subprocess_version` (the `--version` probe behind `lsp.live`, readiness and the adapters) and the ripgrep runner `run_ripgrep` therefore inherited LCI's stdin. Under the MCP stdio transport that is the client's JSON-RPC stream: a child that reads stdin could block (a reproduced 5 s hang of the fake server's `--version` with an open stdin pipe, 0.3 s with null) or consume protocol bytes. Both now set `.stdin(Stdio::null())`, and so does the `kill_pid` helper in lci-maps, whose std `.status()` also inherits. The audit of every product spawn found the rest already null-stdin (`run_limited`, `run_capture`, std `.output()`) or piped on purpose (the LSP transport). Test: tests/cases/child_stdin.rs (suite Lsp) runs the probe in a re-executed child whose stdin is a held-open pipe, with an outer timeout of the loaded budget; it fails 30 s after removing the line from the probe. |

| d514 | 2026-10-22 | DECISION | lsp | N109, N110 (PR 191): `Manager::tree_barrier` returns the sessions it stopped as `Restart { provider, reason }` with reason `TreeChanged`, `NoFileList` or `StampFailed(cause)`; navigation, search and traverse notes say which (`<server> restarted: the tree changed since its last call` unchanged, `... the index has no file list to stamp`, `... the tree stamp failed (<cause>)`); before, every restart claimed a changed tree. The channel-maps `LspMap` status carries the barrier's restarted and kept-session notes, joined with `; ` and followed by the over-budget note, so `fuse_through_maps` reports what the search report does; a healthy kept session therefore makes that map `Partial` with a note. `fuse_through_maps` is test and offline only, so the `Partial` status is not default-reachable, and fusion, labels and run-record readers handle `Partial` as `Ok`. The run-record comparison tests compare notes without barrier notes (which call starts the server decides which carries the kept-session note). Follow-ups: a doc line on `MapStatus::Partial` that it also carries notes the map has no other field for (with the Full gate), and N114. |
